\check{\zeta}^{n,0}_{\xi}\in H^{1}_{{{\operatorname{per}}}}(Q)
as the unique periodic scalar field that satisfies

(2.8)

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\check{\zeta}^{n,0}_{\xi}\,=\,\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}i\xi\check{\zeta}^{n-1,0}_{\xi})+i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\check{\zeta}^{n-1,0}_{\xi}+i\xi\check{\zeta}^{n-2,0}_{\xi}\big)\\
-\check{\psi}^{n+1}_{\xi}+\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}^{n+1-k}_{\xi}}\check{\psi}^{k}_{\xi}\right],

with integration constant

(2.9)

\mathbb{E}\big[\check{\zeta}^{n,0}_{\xi}\big]\,:=\,-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=2}^{k+2}(\overline{\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{l}/\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{2}})\,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}^{k+2-l}_{\xi}}\check{\zeta}^{n-k,0}_{\xi}\right].

With this choice of the constant, the above equation for
\check{\zeta}^{n,0}_{\xi}
is indeed well-posed by the Fredholm alternative as it iteratively ensures that the right-hand side has vanishing average,

(2.10)

\mathbb{E}\left[i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\check{\zeta}^{n-1,0}_{\xi}+i\xi\check{\zeta}^{n-2,0}_{\xi}\big)\right]+\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}^{n+1-k}_{\xi}}\check{\psi}^{k}_{\xi}\right]=0.

Factoring out powers of
i\xi
, we may then define the real-valued symmetric tensor field
\zeta^{n,0}
such that

\zeta^{n,0}\odot(i\xi)^{\otimes(n+1)}\,:=\,\check{\zeta}^{n,0}_{\xi}.\qed

Proof.

It suffices to show by induction that (
2.10
) holds for all
n\geq 0
.
For
k\geq 0
, testing the defining equation (
2.1
) for
\check{\psi}^{k+1}_{\xi}
with
\check{\zeta}^{n,0}_{\xi}
, we get after averaging and integrating by parts,

\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}^{k+1}_{\xi}}(-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\check{\zeta}^{n,0}_{\xi})\right]\\
=\,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}^{k}_{\xi}}\Big(\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}i\xi\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0})+i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}\Big)+\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k-1}}(i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}i\xi)\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}\right]+\sum_{l=2}^{k+1}\overline{\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{l}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k+1-l}}\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}\right].

Now inserting the defining equation (
2.8
) for
\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}
, and reorganizing the terms, this becomes

\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k+1}}\Big(\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}i\xi\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-1,0})+i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\nabla\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-1,0}+i\xi\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-2,0})\Big)+\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k}}(i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}i\xi)\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-1,0}\right]\\
\,=\,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k}}\Big(\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}i\xi\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0})+i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\nabla\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}+i\xi\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-1,0})\Big)+\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k-1}}(i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}i\xi)\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}\right]\\
+\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k+1}}\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{n+1}\right]+\sum_{l=2}^{k+1}\overline{\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{l}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k+1-l}}\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}\right].

Iterating this identity
(n+1)
-times, starting from
k=0
, and using that
\psi^{0}=1
and that
\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{m}\right]=0
for all
m\geq 1
, we deduce after straightforward simplifications

\mathbb{E}\left[i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\nabla\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}+i\xi\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-1,0})\right]+\sum_{k=0}^{n+2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{n+2-k}}\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k}\right]\,=\,-\sum_{k=2}^{n+1}\sum_{l=2}^{k}\overline{\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{l}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k-l}}\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n+1-k,0}\right],

or equivalently,

\mathbb{E}\left[i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\nabla\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n,0}+i\xi\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-1,0})\right]+\sum_{k=0}^{n+2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{n+2-k}}\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k}\right]\\
\,=\,{-}\overline{\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{2}}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-1,0}\big]{-}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\sum_{l=2}^{k+2}\overline{\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{l}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k+2-l}}\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-k-1,0}\right].

Now the choice (
2.9
) of the integration constant for
\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{n-1,0}
precisely ensures that the right-hand side vanishes, which proves that the identity (
2.10
) holds with
n
replaced by
n+1
.
∎

Next, we turn to the construction of corresponding correctors
\{\zeta^{n,m}\}_{n}
for
m\geq 1
, as motivated in (
1.35
). The proof of this lemma is analogous to the one above and is skipped for shortness.

Lemma 2.6
.

Given
m\geq 1
, for all
n\geq 0
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we recursively define
\check{\zeta}^{n,m}_{\xi}\in H^{1}_{{{\operatorname{per}}}}(Q)
as the unique periodic scalar field that satisfies

(2.11)

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\check{\zeta}^{n,m}_{\xi}=\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}i\xi\check{\zeta}^{n-1,m}_{\xi})+i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\nabla\check{\zeta}^{n-1,m}_{\xi}+i\xi\check{\zeta}^{n-2,m}_{\xi})+\check{\zeta}^{n,m-1}_{\xi},

with integration constant

\mathbb{E}\big[\check{\zeta}^{n,m}_{\xi}\big]\,:=\,(\overline{1/\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{2}})\sum_{k=0}^{n+2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}^{n+2-k}_{\xi}}\check{\zeta}^{k,m-1}_{\xi}\right]-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=2}^{k+2}(\overline{\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{l}/\check{\lambda}_{\xi}^{2}})\,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}^{k+2-l}_{\xi}}\check{\zeta}^{n-k,m}_{\xi}\right].

With this choice of the integration constants, the above equation for
\check{\zeta}^{n,m+1}_{\xi}
is indeed well-posed by the Fredholm alternative as it iteratively ensures that the right-hand side has vanishing average,

(2.12)

\mathbb{E}\left[i\xi\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\check{\zeta}^{n-1,m}_{\xi}+i\xi\check{\zeta}^{n-2,m}_{\xi}\big)+\check{\zeta}^{n,m-1}_{\xi}\right]\,=\,0.

In particular,
\mathbb{E}\big[\check{\zeta}_{\xi}^{0,m}\big]=0
for all
m\geq 0
.
Factoring out powers of
i\xi
, we may then define the real-valued symmetric tensor field
\zeta^{n,m}
such that

\zeta^{n,m}\odot(i\xi)^{\otimes(n+1)}\,:=\,\check{\zeta}^{n,m}_{\xi}.\qed

Again, it is useful to further introduce associated flux correctors, which allow us to refine error estimates by directly exploiting cancellations due to fluxes having vanishing average.

Definition 2.7

(Spectral flux correctors)
.

For
n\geq 0
, we define the spectral flux corrector
\tau^{n,0}:=(\tau^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}})_{1\leq i_{1},\ldots,i_{n+1}\leq d}
by

\tau_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}^{n,0}=(\nabla\Phi_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}^{n,0})^{T},

where
\Phi^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)^{d}
is the periodic vector field that satisfies
\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}^{n,0}\big]=0
and

-\triangle\Phi^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}=\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\zeta^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}+\zeta^{n-1,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}e_{i_{n+1}}\big)-\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\zeta^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}+\zeta^{n-1,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}e_{i_{n+1}}\big)\right].

We define the spectral flux corrector
\tau^{n,m}:=(\tau^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}})_{1\leq i_{1},\ldots,i_{n+1}\leq d}
for
n\geq-1
and
m\geq 1
by

\tau_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}^{n,m}=(\nabla\Phi_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}^{n,m})^{T},

where
\Phi^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)^{d}
is the periodic vector field that satisfies
\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}^{n,m}\big]=0
and

-\triangle\Phi^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}=\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\zeta^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}+\zeta^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}e_{i_{n+1}}\big)+\zeta_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+2}}^{n+1,m-1}e_{i_{n+2}}.

(Note that the definition of the
\zeta^{n,m}
’s ensures that the expression in the right-hand side has vanishing average.)
∎

Based on the above definitions, as for (
2.5
), an iterative use of the Poincaré inequality on the unit cell
Q
easily yields the following estimates.

Lemma 2.8
.

For all
m,n\geq 0
,

(2.13)

\|(\psi^{n},\sigma^{n},\rho^{n})\|_{H^{1}(Q)}+|\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}|\leq C^{n},\qquad\|(\zeta^{n,m},\tau^{n,m})\|_{H^{1}(Q)}\lesssim C^{n+m}.\qed

2.2.
Spectral two-scale expansion

Given a smooth function
\bar{w}
, we consider its two-scale expansion of order
\ell\geq 0
associated with the above-defined spectral correctors, as motivated by spectral considerations in Section
1.4
,

(2.14)

S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{w},f]\,:=\,\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\varepsilon^{n}\psi^{n}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{n}\bar{w}\\
+\varepsilon^{3}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-3}(-1)^{m}\varepsilon^{2m}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3-2m}\varepsilon^{n}\zeta^{n,m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f.

We show by PDE techniques that this expansion is indeed well-adapted to describe the local behavior of the solution to the hyperbolic equation in the following sense: the heterogeneous hyperbolic operator applied to
S^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{w},f]
is equivalent to a higher-order effective operator applied to
\bar{w}
, up to error terms of formal order
O(\varepsilon^{\ell})
. The proof is postponed to Section
2.4
.

Proposition 2.9

(Spectral two-scale expansion)
.

Let
\ell\geq 1
,
\varepsilon>0
, and let
\bar{w},f
be smooth functions satisfying

(2.15)

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{w}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{w}\,=\,f.

Then, the associated spectral two-scale expansion of order
\ell
, defined in (
2.14
), satisfies the following relation in the distributional sense in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{w},f]\,=\,f

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{n=\ell}^{2\ell}\varepsilon^{n}\sum_{k=(n-\ell)\vee 1}^{\ell\wedge(n-1)}(-1)^{n-k}\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{n-k}_{i_{k+1}\ldots i_{n}}\psi^{k}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k}}\right]\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}f

\displaystyle\qquad-\varepsilon^{\ell}\nabla\cdot\Big(\rho^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})e_{i_{\ell}}\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}}f\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\varepsilon^{\ell}\nabla\cdot\Big((\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}-\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}})(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\,\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\,\nabla\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}\bar{w}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\varepsilon^{\ell}(\psi^{\ell}+\rho^{\ell})(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{\ell}f-\varepsilon^{\ell}\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}):\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}\bar{w}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{k=\ell+2-n}^{\ell+1}\varepsilon^{n+k-2}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{i_{n+1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}^{n+k-2}\bar{w}

\displaystyle\qquad-\varepsilon^{\ell}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-2}(-1)^{m}\nabla\cdot\Big(\big(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}-\tau^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\big)(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\varepsilon^{\ell}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-2}(-1)^{m}\tau^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}):\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f

\displaystyle\qquad+\varepsilon^{\ell}\partial_{t}^{2}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-3}(-1)^{m}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}\partial_{t}^{2m}f.\qed

2.3.
Well-posedness of homogenized equation

As motivated in Proposition
2.9
, cf. (
2.15
),
we consider the following formal homogenized equation, for
\ell\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{U}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\big)\nabla\bar{U}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}=f,&\text{in $\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}$},\\
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}=f=0,&\text{for $t<0$}.\end{array}\right.

However, as explained in the introduction, the symbol of the operator

(2.18)

-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla

may vanish, so equation (
2.3
) is ill-posed in general.
As described in Section
1.3
, several higher-order modifications of this equation can then be used to ensure well-posedness: high-frequency filtering as in
[
3
]
, higher-order regularization as in
[
5
]
,
or the Boussinesq trick as in
[
1
]
. Let us precisely define each of them:

(I)

High-frequency filtering
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a cut-off function with

\chi|_{\frac{1}{2}B}=1,\qquad\chi|_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B}=0.

Provided that
0<\varepsilon\ll_{\alpha}1
is small enough, the Fourier symbol of the operator (
2.18
) is strictly positive on
\varepsilon^{-\alpha}B
,
and we may then define
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell}
as the unique solution in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
of

(2.19)

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell}=\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)f,

with
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell}=f=0
for
t<0
, such that the spatial Fourier transform of
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell}
is supported in
\mathbb{R}\times\varepsilon^{-\alpha}B
.

(II)

Higher-order regularization
.

Choose
\kappa_{\ell}\geq 0
as the smallest real number such that for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

(2.20)

\qquad\xi\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(i\xi)^{\otimes(k-1)}+\kappa_{\ell}|\xi|^{\ell}\Big)\xi\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda|\xi|^{2}.

Note that (
2.5
) entails
\kappa_{\ell}\leq C^{\ell}
.
Then, for all
\varepsilon>0
, we can define
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}
as the unique solution in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
of

(2.21)

\qquad\quad\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}+\kappa_{\ell}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{\ell}\Big)\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}\,=\,f,

with
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}=f=0
for
t<0
.

(III)

Boussinesq trick
.

Set
\kappa_{1}=1
,
\kappa_{2j}=0
for all
j
, and for
j>0
we define inductively
\kappa_{2j+1}\geq 0
as the smallest value such that for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

(2.22)

\qquad\xi\cdot\Big(\kappa_{2j+1}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\,+\,\sum_{l=1}^{2j}\kappa_{l}\,\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{2j+2-l}\odot(i\tfrac{\xi}{|\xi|})^{2j+1-l}\Big)\xi\,\geq\,0.

Note that (
2.5
) entails
|\kappa_{l}|\leq C^{l}
for all
l
.
Then, for all
\varepsilon>0
, we can define
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}
as the unique solution in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
of

(2.23)

\quad\qquad\partial_{t}^{2}\Big(1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}\\
-\nabla\cdot\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\kappa_{n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(\tfrac{\nabla}{|\nabla|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{n-1}\bigg)\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}\\
\,=\,\Big(1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)f,

with
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}=f=0
for
t<0
.

We analyze these three modifications of the formal homogenized equation (
2.3
) and show that they are well-posed and all equivalent up to higher-order errors.
The proof is postponed to Section
2.5
.

Lemma 2.10

(Well-posedness of effective equation)
.

Let
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and let
\ell\geq 1
.

(i)

If the spatial Fourier transform of
f
is supported in
\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
for some
R\geq 1
, and provided that
\varepsilon R\ll 1
is small enough (independently of
\ell
), then the formal effective equation (
2.3
) admits a unique ancient solution
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R};\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
with spatial Fourier transform supported in
\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
.
Moreover, it satisfies for all
r,t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}D\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t),\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

\displaystyle\|\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle\|f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

(ii)

The modified equations (
2.19
), (
2.21
), and (
2.23
) are well-posed in
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R};\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
in their respective sense, and their solutions satisfy for all
r\geq 0
, for
(\star)=\operatorname{(I)}
or
\operatorname{(II)}
,

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}D\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{{(\star)},\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

\displaystyle\|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle\|f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

and for
(\star)=\operatorname{(III)}
,

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}D\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{r}\langle\varepsilon\nabla\rangle^{\lfloor\frac{\ell-1}{2}\rfloor}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

\displaystyle\|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle\varepsilon\nabla\rangle^{\lfloor\frac{\ell-1}{2}\rfloor}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

(iii)

If the spatial Fourier transform of
f
is supported in
\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
for some
R\geq 1
, and provided that
\varepsilon R\ll 1
is small enough (independently of
\ell
), we have for all
r,t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell;t}-\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{K_{\alpha}\ell+r}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell;t}-\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{\ell+r}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell;t}-\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+r-2}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

with
K_{\alpha}\leq 1/\alpha
in the first estimate.
∎

2.4.
Proof of Proposition
2.9

By scaling, it suffices to consider the case
\varepsilon=1
and we omit the subscript
\varepsilon=1
for notational convenience. The two-scale expansion (
2.14
) can be decomposed as
S^{\ell}[\bar{w},f]=S_{1}^{\ell}[\bar{w}]+S_{2}^{\ell}[f]
, in terms of

\displaystyle S_{1}^{\ell}[\bar{w}]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\psi^{n}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\bar{w},

\displaystyle S_{2}^{\ell}[f]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3-2m}(-1)^{m}\zeta^{n,m}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f.

We split the proof into three steps, separately deriving equations for each part.

Step
1. Equation for
S_{1}^{\ell}[\bar{w}]
: we show that

(2.24)

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)S_{1}^{\ell}[\bar{w}]\,=\,\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\psi^{n}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\bigg(\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{w}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot\nabla^{k-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{w}\bigg)\\
-\nabla\cdot\Big((\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}-\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}})\,\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\,\nabla\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}\bar{w}\Big)-\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}\bar{w}\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{k=\ell+2-n}^{\ell+1}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{i_{n+1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}^{n+k-2}\bar{w}.

A direct calculation based on the general formula
-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla(hg)=(-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla h)g-\nabla\cdot(ah)\cdot\nabla g-a\nabla h\cdot\nabla g-ha:\nabla^{2}g
yields, for all
n\geq 0
,

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\big(\psi^{n}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\bar{w}\big)\\
=\,(-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\bar{w}-\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}e_{i_{n+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\bar{w}\\
-(e_{i_{n+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\bar{w}-(e_{i_{n+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}e_{i_{n+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+2}}\bar{w}.

Combined with the defining equation (
2.1
) for
\psi^{n}
, this entails

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\big(\psi^{n}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\bar{w}\big)\\
=\,\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{n-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\bar{w}-\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}e_{i_{n+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\bar{w}\\
+(e_{i_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\psi^{n-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\bar{w}-(e_{i_{n+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\bar{w}\\
+(e_{i_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{n-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-2}}e_{i_{n-1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\bar{w}-(e_{i_{n+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}e_{i_{n+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+2}}\bar{w}\\
-\sum_{k=2}^{n}(e_{i_{k}}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k-2}}e_{i_{k-1}})\,\psi^{n-k}_{i_{k+1}\ldots i_{n}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\bar{w},

and thus, after summation over
0\leq n\leq\ell
, taking into account the telescoping sum we obtain that

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)S_{1}^{\ell}[\bar{w}]\,=\,\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\Big(\partial_{t}^{2}-\sum_{k=2}^{\ell-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{k-2}}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{k-2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{k-2}}\Big)\bar{w}\\
-\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}e_{i_{\ell+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell+1}}\bar{w}-e_{i_{\ell+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\nabla\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}+\psi^{\ell-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}}e_{i_{\ell}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell+1}}\bar{w}\\
-(e_{i_{\ell+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}e_{i_{\ell+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell+2}}\bar{w},

which we can rewrite as

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)S_{1}^{\ell}[\bar{w}]\,=\,\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\Big(\partial_{t}^{2}-\sum_{k=2}^{\ell+1-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{k-2}}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{k-2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{k-2}}\Big)\bar{w}\\
-\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}e_{i_{\ell+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell+1}}\bar{w}-(e_{i_{\ell+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}e_{i_{\ell+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell+2}}\bar{w}\\
-\Big(e_{i_{\ell+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\nabla\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}+\psi^{\ell-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}}e_{i_{\ell}}\big)-\sum_{k=2}^{\ell+1}(e_{i_{k}}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k-2}}e_{i_{k-1}})\psi^{\ell+1-k}_{i_{k+1}\ldots i_{\ell+1}}\Big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell+1}}\bar{w}.

Recalling the definition of flux correctors, cf. Definition
2.4
, this means

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)S_{1}^{\ell}[\bar{w}]\,=\,\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\Big(\partial_{t}^{2}-\sum_{k=2}^{\ell+1-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{k-2}}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{k-2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{k-2}}\Big)\bar{w}\\
-\nabla\cdot\big((\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}-\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}})e_{i_{\ell+1}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell+1}}\bar{w}\\
-(e_{i_{\ell+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}e_{i_{\ell+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell+2}}\bar{w},

and the claim (
2.24
) follows.

Step
2. Equation for
S_{2}^{\ell}[f]
: we show that

(2.25)

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)S_{2}^{\ell}[f]\,=\,-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\psi^{n}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}f\\
-\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-2}(-1)^{m}\nabla\cdot\Big(\big(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}-\tau^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\Big)\\
-\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-2}(-1)^{m}\tau^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{n+1-k}\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{n+1-k}_{i_{k+1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\psi^{k}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k}}\right]\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}f\\
+\partial_{t}^{2}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-3}(-1)^{m}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}\partial_{t}^{2m}f.

A direct calculation yields for all
m,n
,

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\big(\zeta^{n,m}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\big)\\
\,=\,(-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\zeta^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f-\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}e_{i_{n+2}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+2}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
-(e_{i_{n+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\zeta^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+2}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f-(e_{i_{n+3}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}e_{i_{n+2}})\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+3}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+3}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f.

For
m=0
, inserting the defining equation for
\zeta^{n,0}
, cf. (
2.8
), this entails

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\big(\zeta^{n,0}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}f\big)\\
\,=\,\nabla\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{n-1,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}e_{i_{n+1}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}f-\nabla\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}e_{i_{n+2}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+2}}f\\
+\big(e_{i_{n+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\zeta^{n-1,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}f-\big(e_{i_{n+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\zeta^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+2}}f\\
+\big(e_{i_{n+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{n-2,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}f-\big(e_{i_{n+3}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}e_{i_{n+2}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+3}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+3}}f\\
-\psi^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}f+\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{n+1-k}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{n+1-k}_{i_{k+1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\psi^{k}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k}}\right]\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}f,

and thus, after summation over
0\leq n\leq\ell-3
,

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3}\zeta^{n,0}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}f\,=\,-\nabla\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2}}e_{i_{\ell-1}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}}f\\
-e_{i_{\ell-1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\zeta^{\ell-3,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2}}+\zeta^{\ell-4,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-3}}e_{i_{\ell-2}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}}f\\
-\big(e_{i_{\ell}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2}}e_{i_{\ell-1}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}f\\
-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}f+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3}\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{n+1-k}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{n+1-k}_{i_{k+1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\psi^{k}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k}}\right]\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}f.

Proceeding similarly for
m>0
, we find

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3-2m}\zeta^{n,m}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
\,=\,-\nabla\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}e_{i_{\ell-1-2m}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
-e_{i_{\ell-1-2m}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}+\zeta^{\ell-4-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-3-2m}}e_{i_{\ell-2-2m}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
-\big(e_{i_{\ell-2m}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}e_{i_{\ell-1-2m}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3-2m}\zeta^{n,m-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f.

Combining the above two identities, we are led to

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla S_{2}^{\ell}[f]\,=\,-\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-3}(-1)^{m}\nabla\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}e_{i_{\ell-1-2m}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
-\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-3}(-1)^{m}e_{i_{\ell-1-2m}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}+\zeta^{\ell-4-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-3-2m}}e_{i_{\ell-2-2m}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
-\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-3}(-1)^{m}\big(e_{i_{\ell-2m}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}e_{i_{\ell-1-2m}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}f+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3}\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{n+1-k}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{n+1-k}_{i_{k+1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\psi^{k}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k}}\right]\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}f\\
+\sum_{2m=2}^{\ell-3}(-1)^{m}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3-2m}\zeta^{n,m-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f.

Recalling the definition of flux correctors, cf. Definition
2.7
, as well as (
2.10
) and (
2.12
), and reorganizing the terms, the claim (
2.25
) follows. Note that the flux correctors
\tau^{n,m}
’s are nontrivial even for
n=-1
, but those appear only in the case when
\ell-3
is odd.

Step
3. Conclusion.

Combining the results of the last two steps, reorganizing the terms, and recalling the relation (
2.15
) between
\bar{w},f
, we are led to

(2.26)

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)S^{\ell}[\bar{w},f]\,=\,f\\
-\bigg(\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)^{-1}-1-\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{n+1-k}\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{n+1-k}_{i_{k+1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\psi^{k}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k}}\right]\nabla^{n+1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+1}}\bigg)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)f\\
-\nabla\cdot\Big((\boldsymbol{a}\psi^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}-\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}})\,\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\,\nabla\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}\bar{w}\Big)\\
+\sum_{n=(\ell-1)\vee 1}^{\ell}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}f-\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}\bar{w}\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{k=\ell+2-n}^{\ell+1}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{i_{n+1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}^{n+k-2}\bar{w}\\
-\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-2}(-1)^{m}\nabla\cdot\Big(\big(\boldsymbol{a}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}-\tau^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\big)\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\Big)\\
-\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-2}(-1)^{m}\tau^{\ell-3-2m,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-2-2m}}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
+\partial_{t}^{2}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-3}(-1)^{m}\zeta^{\ell-3-2m,m}\odot\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-2-2m}\partial_{t}^{2m}f,

and it remains to reformulate the second and fourth right-hand side terms.
For the second right-hand side term, we recall the definition (
2.4
) of
\gamma_{\ell}
, which yields

\displaystyle\gamma_{\ell}(\xi)^{-1}-1-\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{n+1-k}}\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[{\Big|\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{n}\Big|^{2}}\bigg]-1-\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{n+1-k}}\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=\ell}^{2\ell}\sum_{k=n-\ell}^{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{n-k}}\check{\psi}_{\xi}^{k}\right].

For the fourth right-hand side term in (
2.26
), we use the auxiliary correctors of Definition
2.4
to write for
\ell\geq 2
,

\psi^{\ell-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}}f\,=\,\rho^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}f-\nabla\cdot\Big(\rho^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}e_{i_{\ell}}\gamma_{\ell}(\nabla)\nabla^{\ell-1}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}}f\Big).

Combining these identities yields the conclusion.∎

2.5.
Proof of Lemma
2.10

We split the proof into five steps.

Step
1. Proof of (i): well-posedness provided
\operatorname{supp}\hat{f}\subset\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
.

In Fourier space, the operator
-\nabla\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1})\nabla
has symbol

\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)\,:=\,\xi\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{{\boldsymbol{b}}}^{k}\odot(i\varepsilon\xi)^{\otimes(k-1)}\Big)\xi.

By Proposition
2.3
, we know that
\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}
is real-valued. Recalling that the uniform ellipticity condition (
1.1
) entails

\lambda|\xi|^{2}\leq\xi\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\xi\leq|\xi|^{2}
after homogenization,
and taking advantage of (
2.5
), we find for
|\xi|\leq R
,

\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)\,\geq\,|\xi|^{2}\Big(\lambda-\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}(\varepsilon C|\xi|)^{k-1}\Big)\,\geq\,|\xi|^{2}\Big(\lambda-\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}(\varepsilon CR)^{k-1}\Big).

Provided that
\varepsilon R\ll 1
is small enough, we deduce for
|\xi|\leq R
,

(2.27)

\tfrac{1}{C}|\xi|^{2}\,\leq\,\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)\,\leq\,C|\xi|^{2}.

We may thus define a solution of (
2.3
) in Fourier space via Duhamel’s formula, that is,

\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}](\xi)\,:=\,\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\sin\big((t-s)\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)^{1/2}\big)}{\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)^{1/2}}\hat{f}^{s}(\xi)\,ds,

where
\mathcal{F}g=\hat{g}
stands for the spatial Fourier transform.
This formula indeed satisfies in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

(2.28)

\partial_{t}^{2}\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]+\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]\,=\,\hat{f},

and thus, upon inverse Fourier transformation, this provides a weak solution
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
of (
2.3
) in
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R};\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. In addition, by construction, the spatial Fourier transform is supported in
\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
as
\hat{f}
is.

We turn to the proof of a priori estimates. As the equation is linear and has constant coefficients, derivatives of the solution satisfy the same equation up to replacing
f
by its corresponding derivatives. It is therefore enough
to prove the stated estimates with
r=0
,

(2.29)

\displaystyle\|D\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t),\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

(2.30)

\displaystyle\|\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle\|f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Moreover, we note that the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-estimate (
2.30
) directly follows from (
2.29
): recalling that
D=(\partial_{t},\nabla)
, we indeed get from (
2.29
) and integration that

\|\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim\,\int_{0}^{t}\|f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,s);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\,ds\,\leq\,\langle t\rangle\|f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

that is, (
2.30
).
It remains to establish (
2.29
). For that purpose, multiplying both sides of equation (
2.28
) by the complex conjugate of
\partial_{t}\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]
and taking the real part, we get

\tfrac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(|\partial_{t}\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]|^{2}+\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}|\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]|^{2}\Big)\,\leq\,\|\hat{f}^{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

which implies

\partial_{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(|\partial_{t}\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]|^{2}+\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}|\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]|^{2}\Big)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\lesssim\,\|f^{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Integrating in time and appealing to (
2.27
), this yields the claim (
2.29
).
Note that uniqueness follows from these a priori estimates by linearity.

For Step 5, we shall also need an a priori estimate for
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
in terms of the
\dot{H}^{-1}
-norm
of the impulse. Multiplying (
2.28
) with the complex conjugate of
(|\cdot|^{2}+\delta)^{-1}\partial_{t}\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]
and repeating the above argument, we infer that

\partial_{t}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|\xi|^{2}+\delta)^{-1}\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}(\xi)\,|\mathcal{F}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}](\xi)|^{2}\,d\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\leq\,\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|\xi|^{2}+\delta)^{-1}|\hat{f}^{t}(\xi)|^{2}\,d\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Integrating in time, using the monotone convergence theorem to pass to the limit
\delta\downarrow 0
, and appealing again to (
2.27
), we deduce

\|\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim\,\|f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

and similarly, due to the constant coefficients and linearity of the equation, we get for all
r\geq 0
,

(2.31)

\|\langle D\rangle^{r}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|\langle D^{r}\rangle f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Step
2. Proof of (ii) for high-frequency filtering.

Let
\alpha,\chi
be fixed. We appeal to (
2.27
) with
R=\varepsilon^{-\alpha}
: provided that
\varepsilon^{1-\alpha}\ll 1
is small enough, we deduce for
|\xi|\leq\varepsilon^{-\alpha}
,

(2.32)

\tfrac{1}{C}|\xi|^{2}\leq\mu^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)\leq C|\xi|^{2}.

Hence, as in Step 1, replacing
f
by
\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)f
, there is a unique solution
u_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
of (
2.19
) in
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R};\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
with spatial Fourier transform supported in
\mathbb{R}\times\varepsilon^{-\alpha}B
, and the claimed a priori estimates similarly follow.

Step
3. Proof of (ii) for higher-order regularization.

In Fourier space, the regularized operator
-\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}+\kappa_{\ell}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{\ell}\big)\nabla

has symbol

\mu^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)\,:=\,\xi\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{{\boldsymbol{b}}}^{k}\odot(i\varepsilon\xi)^{\otimes k-1}+\kappa_{\ell}(\varepsilon|\xi|)^{\ell}\Big)\xi.

Recall that by Proposition
2.3
this symbol is real-valued.
Moreover, the lower bound
\xi\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\xi\geq\lambda|\xi|^{2}
ensures that
\kappa_{\ell}
can indeed be chosen as the smallest value satisfying (
2.20
), while the bound (
2.5
) entails
\kappa_{\ell}\leq C^{\ell}
.
This choice of
\kappa_{\ell}
, together with (
2.5
), yields

\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda|\xi|^{2}\,\leq\,\mu^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)\,\leq\,C^{\ell}|\xi|^{2}\langle\varepsilon\xi\rangle^{\ell}.

We can then solve (
2.21
) in Fourier space again via Duhamel’s formula, and the stated a priori estimates are deduced as in Step 1 using the above coercivity of the regularized symbol. Uniqueness follows by linearity.

Step
4. Proof of (ii) for Boussinesq trick.

In terms of the symbol

(2.33)

\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}(\xi)\,:=\,\frac{\xi\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\big(\kappa_{n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(i\frac{\xi}{|\xi|})^{n-l}\big)(\varepsilon|\xi|)^{n-1}\Big)\xi}{1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\xi|)^{l-1}},

equation (
2.23
) can be written in Fourier space as

(2.34)

\partial_{t}^{2}\mathcal{F}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}]+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}\mathcal{F}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}]=\hat{f}.

Note that (
2.33
) makes sense as
\kappa_{l}\geq 0
for all
l
.
In addition, the choice (
2.22
) of
\{\kappa_{l}\}_{l}
precisely ensures that all the terms of the sum over
n
in the numerator of (
2.33
) are nonnegative (and actually vanish for
n
even due to Proposition
2.3
). Only keeping the term for
n=1
, and recalling
\kappa_{1}=1
by definition, we deduce the lower bound

\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}(\xi)\,\geq\,\frac{\xi\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\xi}{1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\xi|)^{l-1}}\,\geq\,\frac{\lambda|\xi|^{2}}{1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\xi|)^{l-1}},

which is pointwise non-negative.
We can then define a solution of (
2.34
) via Duhamel’s formula

\mathcal{F}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}](\xi)\,=\,\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\sin\big((t-s)\mu^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)^{1/2}\big)}{\mu^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)^{1/2}}\hat{f}^{s}(\xi)\,ds,

and thus, upon inverse Fourier transformation, this provides a weak solution of (
2.23
) in
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R};\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.

We turn to the proof of a priori estimates. As in Step 1, it suffices to establish the estimate in energy norm.
For that purpose, we start from the following equivalent formulation of (
2.34
),

(2.35)

\beta_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\partial_{t}^{2}\mathcal{F}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}]+\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}]\,=\,\beta_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\hat{f},

in terms of the symbols

\displaystyle\beta_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\xi|)^{l-1},

\displaystyle\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\xi\cdot\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\kappa_{n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(i\tfrac{\xi}{|\xi|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\xi|)^{n-1}\bigg)\xi,

with
\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}/\beta_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}=\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}
.
Arguing as in Step 1, and using that
\beta_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}(\xi)\geq 1
and
\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}(\xi)\geq\lambda|\xi|^{2}
, we find that any ancient solution of (
2.35
) satisfies

\|D\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell;t}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim\,\int_{0}^{t}\|(\beta_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Inserting the upper bound

\beta_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}(\xi)\,\leq\,\sum_{l=1}^{\ell}(\varepsilon C|\xi|)^{l-1}\mathds{1}_{l\text{ odd}}\,\leq\,C^{\ell}\langle\varepsilon\xi\rangle^{2\lfloor\frac{\ell-1}{2}\rfloor},

we are led to the claimed a priori estimate on the energy norm.

Step
5. Proof of (iii): comparison of modified equations.

We analyze the differences
\bar{v}^{(\star),\ell}_{\varepsilon}:=\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}-\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
, and we start with
(\star)=\text{(I)}
. By definition, it satisfies the equation

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{v}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{v}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}=(\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)-1)f,

so that (
2.31
) yields for all
r\geq 0
,

\|\langle D\rangle^{r}\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim\,\|\langle D\rangle^{r}(\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)-1)f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

By the properties of the cut-off function
\chi
, we find for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
k\geq 0
,

(2.36)

|\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\xi)-1|\,\leq\,\mathds{1}_{|\varepsilon^{\alpha}\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2}}\,\leq\,(2\varepsilon^{\alpha}|\xi|)^{k}.

Choosing
k={\lfloor\ell/\alpha\rfloor}+1
, we then get by Plancherel’s formula,

\|\langle D\rangle^{r}\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{K_{\alpha}\ell+r}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}

with
K_{\alpha}\leq 1/\alpha
, as claimed.

We turn to the case
(\star)=\operatorname{(II)}
. By definition, the difference
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}
satisfies the following equation,

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}+\kappa_{\ell}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{\ell}\Big)\nabla\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell}\,=\,\kappa_{\ell}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{\ell}\triangle\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}.

Hence, combining (
2.31
) and the a priori estimate of item (ii), together with the bound
\kappa_{\ell}\leq C^{\ell}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}v_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(II)},\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{r}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{\ell}\nabla\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{\ell+r}\nabla\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{\ell+r}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

It remains to treat the case
(\star)=\operatorname{(III)}
. Starting from (
2.3
) and (
2.23
), and recalling
\kappa_{1}=1
, we get the following equation for the corresponding difference,

\partial_{t}^{2}\Big(1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}\\
-\nabla\cdot\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\kappa_{n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(\tfrac{\nabla}{|\nabla|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{n-1}\bigg)\nabla\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}\\
\,=\,\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}(f-\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})\\
+\nabla\cdot\bigg(\sum_{n=2}^{\ell}\Big(\kappa_{n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{l=2}^{n-1}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(\tfrac{\nabla}{|\nabla|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{n-1}\bigg)\nabla\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},

and thus, further using the equation for
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
in the right-hand side, we get after reorganizing the terms,

(2.37)

\partial_{t}^{2}\Big(1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}\\
-\nabla\cdot\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\kappa_{n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(\tfrac{\nabla}{|\nabla|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{n-1}\bigg)\nabla\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}\\
\,=\,-\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\sum_{k=\ell+2-l}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}.

Combining (
2.31
) and the a priori estimate of item (ii), together with the bounds
|{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}|\leq C^{k}
and
\kappa_{\ell}\leq C^{\ell}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{r}\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(III)},\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+r-2}\nabla\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+r-2}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

and the conclusion follows.∎

2.6.
Proof of Theorem
1

Let
\boldsymbol{a}
be
Q
-periodic.
We split the proof into three steps. We first establish (
1.6
) for the energy norm, before turning to the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-estimate, which requires some additional care. We start by assuming that
\operatorname{supp}\hat{f}\subset\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
for some
R\geq 1
, and then conclude with the general case in the last step.

Step
1. Proof of (
1.6
) for the energy norm in case
\operatorname{supp}\hat{f}\subset\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
with
\varepsilon R\ll 1
.

For simplicity, we start by assuming momentarily that the corrector estimates in Lemma
2.8
hold uniformly in the sense of

\displaystyle\|(\psi^{n},\sigma^{n})\|_{{W^{1,\infty}(Q)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{n},

(2.38)

\displaystyle\text{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \|(\zeta^{n,m},\tau^{n,m})\|_{{W^{1,\infty}(Q)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{n+m+1},\qquad\text{for all $n,m\geq 0$.}

As
\operatorname{supp}\hat{f}\subset\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
with
\varepsilon R\ll 1
, we can consider the solution
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
of the formal effective equation (
2.3
) as given by Lemma
2.10
(i).
From Proposition
2.9
and Lemma
B.1
, using the assumed uniform corrector estimate (
2.6
), we then obtain

\|D(u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t},f^{t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}+(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell}D\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

and thus, combining this with the a priori bounds of Lemma
2.10
(i),

(2.39)

\|D(u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t},f^{t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

It remains to replace
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
by
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
in the left-hand side for
(\star)=\text{(I)}
,
(II)
, or
(III)
. For that purpose, recalling the definition of the spectral two-scale expansion, cf. (
2.14
), and using the assumed uniform corrector estimates (
2.6
), we note that

\|D(S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell},f]-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},f])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,C^{\ell}\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{\ell}D(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}-\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

hence, by Lemma
2.10
(iii),

\|D(S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell;t},f^{t}]-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t},f^{t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{C\ell}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Combined with (
2.39
), this proves the claim (
1.6
) for the energy norm in case
\operatorname{supp}\hat{f}\subset\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
with
\varepsilon R\ll 1
, provided that (
2.6
) holds.

It remains to treat the case when the uniform boundedness assumption (
2.6
) for correctors is not satisfied.
In that case, we rather appeal to the Sobolev embedding to estimate products with correctors: for any periodic corrector or corrector gradient
\varphi\in\{\psi^{n},\nabla\psi^{n},\sigma^{n},\nabla\sigma^{n},\zeta^{n,m},\nabla\zeta^{n,m},\tau^{n,m},\nabla\tau^{n,m}\}_{n,m}
, we can estimate, for any function
g
,

(2.40)

\displaystyle\|\varphi(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})g\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\fint_{B_{\varepsilon}(x)}|\varphi(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})|^{2}\Big)\Big(\sup_{B_{\varepsilon}(x)}|g|^{2}\Big)\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(Q)}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\sup_{B_{\varepsilon}(x)}|g|^{2}\Big)\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(Q)}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

provided
a>\frac{d}{2}
.
Up to a fixed loss of derivatives in the estimates, we may then appeal to the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
corrector estimates in Lemma
2.8
, and the above proof of (
1.6
) for the energy norm is adapted directly.

Step
2. Proof of (
1.6
) for the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-norm in case
\operatorname{supp}\hat{f}\subset B_{R}
with
\varepsilon R\ll 1
.

As in Step 1, we aim to apply Proposition
2.9
and Lemma
B.1
, together with corrector estimates. However, the following terms are a priori problematic in the right-hand side of the equation for the spectral two-scale expansion given by Proposition
2.9
,

(2.41)

T_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,:=\,-\varepsilon^{\ell}\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}):\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{k=\ell+2-n}^{\ell+1}\varepsilon^{n+k-2}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{i_{n+1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{2}\nabla_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}^{n+k-2}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}.

Indeed, these terms are not total derivatives and involve
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
itself: when applying Lemma
B.1
to estimate the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-norm of the two-scale expansion error, these terms would therefore contribute like

(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle^{2}\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{C\ell}}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}

with a prefactor
\langle t\rangle^{2}
instead of
\langle t\rangle
.
In order to improve on this, we shall reformulate
T_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
as a total time-derivative up to terms that depend only locally on
f
.
Using the short-hand notation

\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,:=\,-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla,

the effective equation for
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
entails

\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\nabla^{2}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}=-\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{2}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}+\nabla^{2}f.

As in the proof of Lemma
2.10
(i), cf. (
2.27
), the assumption
\varepsilon R\ll 1
precisely ensures that the operator
\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}:\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\dot{H}^{-2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be inverted when restricted to functions with spatial Fourier transform supported in
B_{R}
. As by definition both
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
and
f
have spatial Fourier transform supported in
B_{R}
, we may then write

(2.42)

\nabla^{2}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}=-\partial_{t}^{2}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})^{-1}\nabla^{2}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}+(\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})^{-1}\nabla^{2}f.

By uniform ellipticity (
2.27
), we have for any function
g
with
\operatorname{supp}\hat{g}\subset B_{R}
,

(2.43)

\|(\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})^{-1}\nabla^{2}g\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim\,\|g\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Now using (
2.42
) to reformulate
T_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
, cf. (
2.41
), we get

T_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,=\,\varepsilon^{\ell}\partial_{t}^{2}\Big(\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}):\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})^{-1}\nabla^{2}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\Big)\\
-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{k=\ell+2-n}^{\ell+1}\varepsilon^{n+k-2}\partial_{t}^{2}\Big(\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{i_{n+1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}^{n+k-2}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})^{-1}\nabla^{2}\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\Big)\\
-\varepsilon^{\ell}\sigma^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}):\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla^{\ell}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})^{-1}\nabla^{2}f\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{k=\ell+2-n}^{\ell+1}\varepsilon^{n+k-2}\psi^{n}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k-1}_{i_{n+1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}:\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)\nabla_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n+k-2}}^{n+k-2}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})^{-1}\nabla^{2}f.

Using this to replace the corresponding terms in the equation for the two-scale expansion error in Proposition
2.9
, appealing to Lemma
B.1
to estimate its
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-norm, using the corrector estimates of Lemma
2.8
, using the Sobolev embedding to estimate products with correctors as in (
2.40
), and using (
2.43
), we get for
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

\|u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t},f^{t}]\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}+(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a}D\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

and thus, by the a priori estimate of Lemma
2.10
(i),

(2.44)

\|u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t},f^{t}]\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

It remains to argue as in Step 1 to replace
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
by
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
in the left-hand side.
For that purpose, recalling the definition of the spectral two-scale expansion, cf. (
2.14
), and using again the corrector estimates of Lemma
2.8
together with the Sobolev embedding as in (
2.40
), we note that for
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

\|S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell},f]-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},f]\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,C^{\ell}\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{\ell+a}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}-\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

hence, by Lemma
2.10
(iii),

\|S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell;t},f^{t}]-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t},f^{t}]\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{C\ell}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Combined with (
2.44
), this proves the claim (
1.6
) for the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-norm in case
\operatorname{supp}\hat{f}\subset B_{R}
with
\varepsilon R\ll 1
.

Step
3. Approximation argument: proof for general
f
.

For
R\geq 1
, consider the truncated impulse

f_{R}\,:=\,\chi(\tfrac{1}{R}\nabla)f,

and let
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon,R}^{(\star),\ell}
be the solution of the modified effective equations given by Lemma
2.10
(ii) with impulse
f
replaced by
f_{R}
.
Recalling the definition of the spectral two-scale expansion, cf. (
2.14
), and using again corrector estimates, we then note that for
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

\|D(S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon,R}^{(\star),\ell},f_{R}]-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell},f])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\,\leq\,C^{\ell}\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{\ell+a}D(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}-\bar{u}_{\varepsilon,R}^{(\star),\ell})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{\ell+a}(f-f_{R})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and thus, by linearity and by the a priori estimates of Lemma
2.10
(ii),

\|D(S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon,R}^{(\star),\ell;t},f_{R}^{t}]-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell;t},f^{t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,C^{\ell}\|{\langle D\rangle^{C\ell}}(f-f_{R})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

By definition of
f_{R}
, as in (
2.36
), the right-hand side can now be estimated as follows, for any
k\geq 0
,

\|D(S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon,R}^{(\star),\ell;t},f_{R}^{t}]-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell;t},f^{t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,R^{-k}C^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{C\ell+k}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Combining this with the results of Steps 1 and 2, the conclusion follows for instance with the choice
R=\varepsilon^{-1/2}
and
k=2\ell
.
∎

2.7.
Proof of Corollary
1

The bound (
1.7
) is obtained along the same line as Theorem
1
(using a straightforward adaptation of Lemma
B.1
),
and it remains to deduce (
1.8
).
For that purpose, we note that (
1.8
) would actually follow from (
1.7
) together with the bound

(2.45)

\int_{-\infty}^{t}(t-s)\|\langle D\rangle^{C\ell}f^{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,ds\,\leq\,{C_{f}^{\ell}},

in favor of which we presently argue.
Recall that we assume here
f^{t}(x)=f_{1}(t)f_{2}(x)
, where
f_{1}
has a smooth and compactly supported Fourier transform on
\mathbb{R}
and where
f_{2}
has a compactly supported Fourier transform on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
The assumption on
f_{2}
entails
\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{C\ell}f_{2}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq{C_{f_{2}}^{\ell}}
, while the assumption on
f_{1}
yields for
s\leq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\langle\partial_{t}\rangle^{C\ell}f_{1}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((-\infty,s))}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\langle s\rangle^{-2}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{3}\langle\partial_{t}\rangle^{C\ell}f_{1}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}((-\infty,s))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\langle s\rangle^{-2}C^{\ell}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{C\ell}\langle\partial\rangle^{3}\hat{f}_{1}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}((-\infty,s))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\langle s\rangle^{-2}{C_{f_{1}}^{\ell}},

where
\hat{f}_{1}
stands for the temporal Fourier transform of
f_{1}
.
The claim (
2.45
) follows.
∎

2.8.
Proof of Theorem
3

We briefly explain how the above proof of Theorem
1
is adapted to the random setting.
As for Theorem
1
, we may assume
\operatorname{supp}\hat{f}\subset\mathbb{R}\times B_{R}
with
\varepsilon R\ll 1
, and the general conclusion follows by approximation.
As explained in Appendix
A
, the only difference with respect to the periodic setting is that only a finite number
\ell_{*}=\lceil\frac{\beta\wedge d}{2}\rceil
of correctors can be defined with stationary gradient, and the highest-order corrector has a nontrivial sublinear growth.
Using Proposition
2.9
in combination with Lemma
B.1
to estimate the energy norm of the two-scale expansion error of order
\ell\leq\ell_{*}
, and using the corrector estimates of Appendix
A
and the Sobolev embedding, we find for
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

\|D(u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t},f^{t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega;\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\\
\,\lesssim\,\varepsilon^{\ell}\|\mu_{\ell}^{*}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\gamma(\varepsilon\nabla)\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\\
+\varepsilon^{\ell}\|\mu_{\ell}^{*}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\gamma(\varepsilon\nabla)\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a}D\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

where the weight
\mu_{\ell}^{*}
originates in the growth of correctors and is defined in (
A.1
).
A novelty with respect to the proof of Theorem
1
is that we now need weighted energy estimates for
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
with sublinear weight
\mu_{\ell}^{*}
.
For that purpose, as the homogenized equation (
2.3
) has constant coefficients,
we note that
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
displays ballistic transport, and therefore

\displaystyle\|\mu_{\ell}^{*}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\gamma(\varepsilon\nabla)\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a}D\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu_{\ell}^{*}(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle t\rangle)\,\|\langle\cdot\rangle\gamma(\varepsilon\nabla)\langle D\rangle^{C}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu_{\ell}^{*}(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle t\rangle)\,\|\langle\cdot\rangle\langle D\rangle^{C}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

This is easily obtained by interpolation, using that the weight
x
corresponds to a derivative in Fourier space and using the Duhamel formula for
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
; see e.g.
[
5
, Proof of Proposition 3]
for the details.
The above then becomes

\|D(u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell;t},f^{t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega;\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\,\lesssim\,\varepsilon^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\,\mu_{\ell}^{*}(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle t\rangle)\,\|\langle\cdot\rangle\langle D\rangle^{C}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Note that the error estimate for
\ell=\ell_{*}-1
is occasionally better than the one for
\ell=\ell_{*}
.
Optimizing between the results for
\ell=\ell_{*}
and for
\ell=\ell_{*}-1
, we easily conclude

\|D(u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell_{*}}[\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell_{*};t},f^{t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega;\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\,\lesssim\,\varepsilon^{\ell_{*}}\langle t\rangle\big(\mu_{\ell_{*}}^{*}(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle t\rangle)\wedge\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big)\,\|\langle\cdot\rangle\langle D\rangle^{C}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

As in the proof of Theorem
1
, we can replace
\bar{U}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell_{*}}
in the left-hand side by the solution of any well-posed modification of the formal homogenized equation, and we can derive a similar estimate for the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-norm.
∎

3.
Geometric approach and hyperbolic two-scale expansion

This section is devoted to the definition of hyperbolic correctors and to the proof of Theorem
2
, including the well-posedness of the formal homogenized equation (
1.10
).
This essentially constitutes a rewriting of
[
3
,
24
,
1
]
and is needed to rigorously relate those works to the spectral two-scale expansion, cf. Section
4
.

3.1.
Definition of hyperbolic correctors

We start with the definition of the hyperbolic correctors
\{\phi^{n,m}\}_{n,m}
and of the homogenized tensors
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\}_{n,m}
, as motivated in Section
1.5
.

Definition 3.1

(Hyperbolic correctors)
.

In the periodic setting, the hyperbolic correctors
\{\phi^{n,m}\}_{n,m\geq 0}
, homogenized tensors
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\}_{n\geq 1,m\geq 0}
, and fluxes
\{q^{n,m}\}_{n,m\geq 0}
are inductively defined as follows:

\bullet

We set
\phi^{0,0}:=1
and
\phi^{0,m}:=0
for
m\geq 1
, while for
n\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
we define
\phi^{n,m}:=(\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq d}
with
\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,m}\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)
the periodic scalar field that has vanishing average
\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,m}\big]=0
and satisfies

\quad-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,m}\,=\,\nabla\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,m}e_{j_{n}}\big)+e_{j_{n}}\cdot q^{n-1,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}.

\bullet

For
n\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
, we define
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}:=(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n-1}\leq d}
as the matrix-valued
(n-1)
th-order tensor given by

\quad\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j}\,:=\,\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}j}^{n,m}+\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,m}e_{j}\big)\right].

\bullet

For
n,m\geq 0
, we define
q^{n,m}:=(q^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq d}
with
q^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\in\operatorname{L}^{2}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)^{d}
the periodic vector field given by

q^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\,:=\,\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,m}+\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,m}e_{j_{n}}\big)-\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}j}^{n+1,m-2}e_{j}-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n,m}e_{j_{n}},

where the definition of
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}
ensures
\mathbb{E}\left[q^{n,m}\right]=0
.

In particular, there holds
\phi^{n,m}=0
,
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}=0
, and
q^{n,m}=0
whenever
m
is an odd integer.
3
3

3

This is natural as time derivatives should always come in even numbers in view of equation (
1.2
).

∎

For all
n
, we note that
\phi^{n,0}
coincides with the elliptic corrector of order
n
, cf.
[
6
]
.
As
is
common in the elliptic setting, it is useful to further introduce suitable flux correctors, which indeed allow us to refine error estimates and slightly improve on the result of
[
3
]
. More precisely, flux correctors are designed to allow a direct optimal exploitation of cancellations due to fluxes having vanishing average
\mathbb{E}\big[q^{n,m}\big]=0
.
We start with the definition of flux correctors for
m\geq 1
.

Definition 3.2

(Hyperbolic flux correctors)
.

For
n\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
, we define the hyperbolic flux corrector
\sigma^{n,m}:=(\sigma^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq d}
by

\sigma^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}=(\nabla\Phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})^{\prime},

where
\Phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)^{d}
is the periodic vector field that satisfies

\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,m}\big]=0
and

-\triangle\Phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\,=\,q^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}.\qed

In case
m=0
, as correctors
\phi^{n,0}
coincide with elliptic correctors, they display more structure than general hyperbolic correctors. We recall how this structure can be exploited to construct a suitable flux corrector
\sigma^{n,0}
that is skew-symmetric: the following lemma is essentially borrowed from
[
15
]
.

Lemma 3.3

(Elliptic correctors and flux correctors)
.

Up to symmetrization of indices, the elliptic correctors
\{\phi^{n,0}\}_{n\geq 0}
and homogenized tensors
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}\}_{n\geq 1}
coincide with the modified families
\{\tilde{\phi}^{n,0}\}_{n\geq 0}
and
\{\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}\}_{n\geq 1}
defined via the following cell problems:

\bullet

We set
\tilde{\phi}^{0,0}:=1
and for
n\geq 1
we define
\tilde{\phi}^{n,0}:=(\tilde{\phi}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots j_{n},\leq d}
with
\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0}\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)
the periodic scalar field that has vanishing average
\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0}\big]=0
and satisfies

\quad-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0}\,=\,\nabla\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{a}\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,0}e_{j_{n}}\big)+e_{j_{n}}\cdot\tilde{q}^{n-1,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}.

\bullet

For
n\geq 1
, we define
\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}:=(\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n-1}\leq d}
with
\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}
the matrix given by

\quad\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j}\,:=\,\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}j}^{n,0}+\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,0}e_{j}\big)\right].

\bullet

For
n\geq 0
, we define
\tilde{q}^{n,0}:=(\tilde{q}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq d}
with
\tilde{q}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\in\operatorname{L}^{2}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)^{d}
the periodic vector field given by

\quad\tilde{q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0}:=\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0}+\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,0}e_{j_{n}}\big)-\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}}-\sigma_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,0}e_{j_{n}},

where the definition of
\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}
and
\sigma^{n-1,0}
ensures
\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{q}^{n,0}\right]=0
and
\nabla\cdot\tilde{q}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}=0
.

\bullet

We set
\sigma^{0,0}:=0
and we define the flux corrector
\sigma^{n,0}:=(\sigma^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq d}
for
n\geq 1
with
\sigma^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)^{d\times d}
the periodic skew-symmetric matrix field that has vanishing average
\mathbb{E}\big[\sigma^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\big]=0
and satisfies

\quad-\triangle\sigma^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}=\nabla\times\tilde{q}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}},\qquad\nabla\cdot\sigma^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}=\tilde{q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0},

where we use the vectorial notation
(\nabla\times F)_{ij}:=\nabla_{i}F_{j}-\nabla_{j}F_{i}
for a vector field
F
.

More precisely, these modified correctors coincide with
\{\phi^{n,0}\}_{n\geq 0}
in the sense that we have for all
n\geq 0
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\phi^{n,0}\odot\xi^{\otimes n}\,=\,\tilde{\phi}^{n,0}\odot\xi^{n},\qquad(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}\odot\xi^{\otimes(n-1)})\xi\,=\,(\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}\odot\xi^{\otimes(n-1)})\xi.\qed

Proof.

We refer to
[
17
]
or
[
15
]
for the construction of the skew-symmetric flux corrector
\sigma^{n,0}
, based on the compatibility condition
\nabla\cdot\tilde{q}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}=0
,
and we now turn to the equivalence of
\phi^{n,0}
and
\tilde{\phi}^{n,0}
.
Setting

(3.1)

\hat{q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0}:=\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0}+\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,0}e_{j_{n}}\big)-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}}-\sigma^{n-1,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}},

the equation for
\phi^{n,0}
in Definition
3.1
for
n\geq 1
can be written as

(3.2)

-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,0}\,=\,\nabla\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,0}e_{j_{n}}\big)+e_{j_{n}}\cdot\big(\hat{q}^{n-1,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}+\sigma_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-2}}^{n-2,0}e_{j_{n-1}}\big).

Symmetrizing indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}
, the skew-symmetry of
\sigma^{n-2,0}
allows us to drop the corresponding right-hand side term, and we may conclude by induction that
\phi^{n,0}
coincides with its modified version
\tilde{\phi}^{n,0}
up to symmetrization as stated.
∎

An iterative use of the Poincaré inequality on the unit cell
Q
ensures the well-posedness of the above objects and further provides the following a priori estimates.

Lemma 3.4
.

In the periodic setting, the above quantities
\{\phi^{n,m},\sigma^{n,m},\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m})_{n,m}
are uniquely defined and satisfy for all
n,m\geq 0
,

\|(\phi^{n,m},\sigma^{n,m})\|_{H^{1}(Q)}+|\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}|\,\leq\,C^{n+m}.\qed

We conclude this paragraph with some important vanishing property of the higher-order hyperbolic homogenized coefficients, which extends the corresponding elliptic result
[
15
, Lemma 2.3]
; see also
[
5
, Proposition 1]
,
[
3
, Theorem 2.13]
, and
[
1
, Theorem 3.5]
. The proof is postponed to Section
3.5
.

Proposition 3.5

(Symmetry of homogenized coefficients)
.

For all
n\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
, there holds for any
j_{1},\ldots,j_{n+1}
,

e_{j_{n+1}}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}}\,=\,(-1)^{n+1}e_{j_{1}}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{j_{n+1}\ldots j_{3}}e_{j_{2}}.

In particular, whenever
n
is even, we have

\xi\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\odot\xi^{\otimes(n-1)})\xi=0,\qquad\text{for all $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.\qed

3.2.
Geometric two-scale expansion

Given a smooth function
\bar{w}
, we consider its two-scale expansion associated with the above-defined hyperbolic correctors, as in (
1.37
),

(3.3)

H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{w}]\,:=\,\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\varepsilon^{n+m}\phi^{n,m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w},

and we show that it is well-adapted to describe the local behavior of the solution to the hyperbolic equation in the following sense: as explained in (
1.39
), the heterogeneous hyperbolic operator applied to
H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{w}]
is equivalent to some higher-order effective operator applied to
\bar{w}
(up to
O(\varepsilon^{\ell})
error terms).
The proof is postponed to Section
3.6
.

Proposition 3.6

(Geometric two-scale expansion)
.

Let
\ell\geq 1
,
\varepsilon>0
, and let
\bar{w},f
be smooth functions satisfying

(3.4)

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{w}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{m}\Big)\nabla\bar{w}\,=\,f.
