Then, the associated geometric two-scale expansion of order
\ell
, defined in (
3.3
), satisfies the following relation in the distributional sense in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\big(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\nabla\big)H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{w}]\\
=\,f-\varepsilon^{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\nabla\cdot\Big(\big(\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}-\sigma^{n,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\big)(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\Big)\hskip 99.58464pt\\
+\varepsilon^{\ell}\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\partial_{t}\Big(\phi^{n,\ell-n}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{\ell+1-n}\bar{w}-\sigma^{n-1,\ell+1-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}):\nabla^{2}\nabla^{n-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\Big).\qed

Remark 3.7
.

Note that the last two right-hand side terms in the above equation for the two-scale expansion
H_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{w}]
are total derivatives (with respect to time or space):
this is not a trivial fact for the last term, as it is based on the possibility of constructing skew-symmetric flux correctors
\{\sigma^{n,0}\}_{n}
. This happens to be crucial when applying Lemma
B.1
in order to deduce an optimal
\operatorname{L}^{2}
error estimate. This slightly refines the analysis of
[
3
]
.
∎

3.3.
Homogenized equations and secular growth problem

As motivated in Proposition
3.6
, cf. (
3.4
), we consider the following formal homogenized equation, for
\ell\geq 1
,

(3.5)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{W}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\nabla\cdot\big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{m}\big)\nabla\bar{W}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}=f,&\text{in $\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}$},\\
\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}=f=0,&\text{for $t<0$}.\end{array}\right.

However, this equation mixes higher-order space and time derivatives, and its well-posedness is problematic.
To avoid the secular growth problem described in Section
1.3
, we follow
[
3
]

and first show that the differential operator in (
3.5
) can be rewritten in such a way that it does no longer mix space and time derivatives. This is achieved by iteratively using the equation to eliminate time derivatives up to higher-order terms, which is referred to as the ‘criminal method’ in
[
3
]
.
The proof is postponed to Section
3.7
.
Note that the new homogenized coefficients
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\}_{n}
in this reformulation
automatically coincide with the coefficients given by the spectral approach: this can for instance be deduced a posteriori by comparing the corresponding homogenization results, Theorems
1
and
2
; this allows us to use already here the same notation
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\}_{n}
as for spectral homogenized coefficients.
Note that by definition
\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{1}=\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{1,0}=\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}
.

Lemma 3.8

(Revamped homogenized equation)
.

Given
\ell\geq 1
and
\varepsilon>0
, if
\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},f
are smooth and satisfy the formal homogenized equation (
3.5
), then we have

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,=\,f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f+\nabla\cdot E_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},

where the coefficients
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n},\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\}_{n}
and the remainder
E_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
are as follows:

\bullet

We define the matrix-valued symmetric tensor
\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{p}:=(\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{p}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p-1}})_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{p-1}\leq d}
for
p\geq 1

such that for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\xi\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{p}\odot\xi^{\otimes(p-1)})\xi\,:=\,\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\sum_{(m_{1},\ldots,m_{k})\in I_{k}}\sum_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{k}\geq 1\atop k+|n|=p+1}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\xi\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot\xi^{\otimes(n_{j}-1)}\big)\xi,

in terms of the index set

I_{k}\,:=\,\Big\{m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k}):m_{j}\geq 0\penalty\ \forall j,\penalty\ \sum_{j=1}^{s}m_{j}\geq 2s\penalty\ \forall s<k,\penalty\ |m|=2(k-1)\Big\}.

\bullet

We define the matrix-valued symmetric tensor
\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{p}:=(\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{p}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p-1}})_{0\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{p-1}\leq d}
for all
p\geq 1
such that for all
\hat{\xi}=(\xi_{0},\xi)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\quad\xi\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{p}\odot\hat{\xi}^{\otimes(p-1)})\xi:=\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{(m_{1},\ldots,m_{k})\in J_{k}}\sum_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{k}\geq 1\atop|n|+|m|=p+k+1}\xi_{0}^{|m|-2k}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\xi\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot\xi^{\otimes(n_{j}-1)}\big)\xi,

in terms of the index set

J_{k}\,:=\,\Big\{m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k}):m_{j}\geq 0\penalty\ \forall j,\,\sum_{j=1}^{s}m_{j}\geq 2s\penalty\ \forall s\leq k\Big\}.

\bullet

For
\varepsilon\ll 1
small enough, the error term
E_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
satisfies pointwise, for all
r\geq 0
,

|\langle D\rangle^{r}E_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}|\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C\ell)^{\ell}\Big(\big|\langle D\rangle^{r+\ell}\langle\varepsilon CD\rangle^{\ell^{2}}D\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\big|+\big|\langle D\rangle^{r+\ell-1}\langle\varepsilon CD\rangle^{\ell^{2}}f\big|\Big).

In particular, Lemma
3.4
implies for all
p\geq 1
,

(3.6)

|\bar{{\boldsymbol{b}}}^{p}|+|\bar{{\boldsymbol{c}}}^{p}|\leq C^{p},

and Proposition
3.5
entails, whenever
p
is an even integer,

\xi\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{p}\odot\xi^{\otimes(p-1)})\xi=0,\qquad\text{for all $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$.}\qed

The above thus leads us to considering the following modified version of the formal effective equation (
3.5
),

(3.7)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{V}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\big)\nabla\bar{V}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}&\\
\hskip 85.35826pt=f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\big(\sum_{k=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{k-1}\big)\nabla f,&\text{in $\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}$},\\
\bar{V}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}=f=0,&\text{for $t<0$}.\end{array}\right.

As with the spectral approach, the symbol of the operator
-\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\big)\nabla
lacks positivity, so this equation is ill-posed in general.
To cure this issue, we argue exactly as in Section
2.3
: we can consider several well-posed higher-order modifications of this equation, either by high-frequency filtering, by higher-order regularization, or by the Boussinesq trick, and we denote by
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell},\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell},\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}
the corresponding solutions, respectively.
We refer to Lemma
2.10
for the details (up to replacing the impulse
f
in (
2.3
) by the specific right-hand side in (
3.7
)).

Next, we show that the above modification procedure
\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\mapsto\bar{V}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
for the formal effective equation can be inverted: more precisely, the solution
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
of any of the well-posed modifications of (
3.7
) is an approximate solution of the formal effective equation (
3.5
) up to an
O(\varepsilon^{\ell})
error.

Lemma 3.9

(Inversion procedure)
.

Given
\ell\geq 1
and
\varepsilon>0
, if
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
is the solution of one of the well-posed modifications of equation (
3.7
) as given by Lemma
2.10
,
then we have

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{v}^{(\star),\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{m}\Big)\nabla\bar{v}^{(\star),\ell}_{\varepsilon}=f+\nabla\cdot F_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell},

where the error
\nabla\cdot F_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
satisfies for all
r\geq 0
,

\|\langle D\rangle^{r}F_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell;t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C\ell)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{r+C\ell}\langle\varepsilon CD\rangle^{\ell^{2}}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

where the constant
C
further depends on the choice of
\alpha
in case
(\star)=\operatorname{(I)}
.
∎

3.4.
Proof of Theorem
2

Applying Proposition
3.6
with
\bar{w}=\bar{v}^{(\star),\ell}_{\varepsilon}
,
appealing to Lemma
3.9
, and comparing with the solution
u_{\varepsilon}
of the heterogeneous wave equation (
1.2
), we find

\big(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\nabla\big)(u_{\varepsilon}-H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}])\,=\,-\nabla\cdot F_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}\\
+\varepsilon^{\ell}\sum_{n=1}^{\ell+1}\nabla\cdot\Big(\big(\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,\ell-n+1}-\sigma^{n-1,\ell-n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}\big)(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\nabla\nabla^{n-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n+1}\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}\Big)\\
-\varepsilon^{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\partial_{t}\Big(\big(\phi^{n+1,\ell-n-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}ji}e_{j}\otimes e_{i}-\sigma^{n-1,\ell-n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}\big)(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}):\nabla^{2}\nabla^{n-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}\Big).

By the a priori estimates of Lemma
B.1
, using corrector estimates of Lemma
3.4
, and using the Sobolev embedding to estimate products with correctors as in (
2.40
), we get for
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

\|u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-H_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell;t}]\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|D(u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-H_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell;t}])\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\,\lesssim\,\|\langle D\rangle F_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}+(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{\ell+a+1}D\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Combining this bound with the estimate of Lemma
3.9
on the remainder
F_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
, and with the a priori estimates of Lemma
2.10
(ii) for
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
, the conclusion follows.
∎

3.5.
Proof of Proposition
3.5

We split the proof into two steps.

Step
1. Migration process: proof that for all
n,m,p,q\geq 0
,

(3.8)

\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}-\phi^{p-1,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p-1}}e_{j_{p}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\big)\right]\\
=-\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{p+1,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}i_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}-\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}e_{i_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-2,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-2}}e_{i_{n-1}}\big)\right]\\
-\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}\phi^{n,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}+\phi^{p+1,q-2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}i_{n}}\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}\big)\right],

and in addition, for all
n,m,q\geq 0
,

(3.9)

\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,q}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right]\\
+(-1)^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla\phi^{n,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{0,m}e_{i_{1}}+\phi^{n-1,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{3}}e_{i_{2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{0,m}e_{i_{1}}\right].

The equation for
\phi^{n,m}
in Definition
3.1
yields

\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\right]\,=\,-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\right]\\
+\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}e_{i_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}+\phi^{n-2,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-2}}e_{i_{n-1}}\big)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}\phi^{n,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}\right],

while the equation for
\phi^{{p+1},q}
leads to

\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}e_{i_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}\right]\,=\,-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla\phi^{p+1,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}i_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}\right]\\
+\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{p,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}}+\phi^{p-1,q}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p-1}}e_{j_{p}}\big)\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{p+1,q-2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{p}i_{n}}\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}\right].

Summing these two identities, the claim (
3.8
) easily follows.
Next, using (
3.8
) in form of

\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,q}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right]=-\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k-1,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+3}}\phi^{n-k+2,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\right]\\
-\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{k-1,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+3}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n-k+2,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}-\phi^{k-2,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots j_{n-k+4}}e_{j_{n-k+3}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-k+1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}e_{i_{n-k+2}}\big)\right]\\
-\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{k,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n-k+1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}-\phi^{k-1,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+3}}e_{i_{n-k+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-k,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k}}e_{i_{n-k+1}}\big)\right],

and summing this identity for
1\leq k\leq n
, we find after straightforward simplifications

\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,q}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right]=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k-1,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+3}}\phi^{n-k+2,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\right]\\
-(-1)^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{n,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{1,m}_{i_{1}}-\phi^{n-1,q+2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{3}}e_{i_{2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{0,m}e_{i_{1}}\big)\right].

Using the equation for
\phi^{1,m}
, the second claim (
3.9
) easily follows.

Step
2. Conclusion.

For
n\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
, the definition of
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}
and the equation for
\phi^{1,0}
yield

e_{j}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\,=\,-\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{1,0}_{j}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n}}-e_{j}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-1,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\big)\right].

Iterating identity (
3.8
) then leads to

e_{j}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\,=\,(-1)^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{n,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{1,m}_{i_{1}}-\phi^{n-1,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{3}}e_{i_{2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{0,m}e_{i_{1}}\big)\right]\\
-\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right],

hence, using the equation for
\phi^{1,m}
,

(3.10)

e_{j}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\,=\,(-1)^{n+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{n,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{2}}+\phi^{n-1,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{3}}e_{i_{2}}\big)\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{0,m}e_{i_{1}}\right]\\
-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right].

For
m=0
, this already yields the conclusion

e_{j}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\,=\,(-1)^{n+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\nabla\phi^{n,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{2}}+\phi^{n-1,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{3}}e_{i_{2}}\big)\cdot\boldsymbol{a}e_{i_{1}}\right]\,=\,(-1)^{n+1}e_{i_{1}}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{3}}e_{i_{2}}.

For
m\geq 2
, identity (
3.10
) rather takes the form

(3.11)

e_{j}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\,=\,-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right],

which we shall combine with an iterative use of identity (
3.9
). More precisely, in case
m=4m^{\prime}+2
with an integer
m^{\prime}\geq 0
, iterating identity (
3.9
) (starting from
q=0
with
m
replaced by
m-2=4m^{\prime}
), and recalling
\phi^{0,l}=0
for
l\geq 1
, we find

\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,0}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,m-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,2m^{\prime}}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,2m^{\prime}}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right],

and thus, combining this with (
3.11
), and replacing
k
by
n-k+1
in the sum,

e_{j}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}\,=\,-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,2m^{\prime}}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,2m^{\prime}}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right]\,=\,(-1)^{n+1}e_{i_{1}}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{3}}e_{i_{2}}.

Similarly, in the case
m=4m^{\prime}
with integer
m^{\prime}\geq 1
, we find

\displaystyle e_{j}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-1}}e_{i_{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,2m^{\prime}}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,2m^{\prime}-2}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{k,2m^{\prime}-2}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{n-k+2}}\phi^{n-k+1,2m^{\prime}}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{n-k+1}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{n+1}e_{i_{1}}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}_{ji_{n}\ldots i_{3}}e_{i_{2}},

and the conclusion follows.
∎

3.6.
Proof of Proposition
3.6

We focus on the case
\varepsilon=1
and drop it from all subscripts in the notation, while the final result is obtained after
\varepsilon
-rescaling.
Since the correctors
\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,m}
do not depend on time, a direct calculation yields for all
n,m\geq 0
,

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)\big(\phi^{n,m}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\big)\,=\,\phi^{n,m}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{m+2}\bar{w}\\
+(-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}-\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}e_{j_{n+1}})\nabla^{n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\\
-(e_{j_{n+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})\nabla^{n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}-(e_{j_{n+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}e_{j_{n+1}})\nabla^{n+2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+2}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}.

Inserting the defining equation for the hyperbolic corrector
\phi^{n,m}
, cf. Definition
3.1
, we get for all
n\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
,

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)\big(\phi^{n,m}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\big)\,=\,\phi^{n,m}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{m+2}\bar{w}-\phi^{n,m-2}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\\
+\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-1,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}})\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}-\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}e_{j_{n+1}})\nabla^{n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\\
+(e_{j_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n-1,m})\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}-(e_{j_{n+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})\nabla^{n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\\
+(e_{j_{n}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-2}}^{n-2,m}e_{j_{n-1}})\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}-(e_{j_{n+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}e_{j_{n+1}})\nabla^{n+2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+2}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\\
-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n-1,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-2}}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{n-2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-2}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w},

and thus, after summation over
1\leq n\leq\ell
and
0\leq m\leq\ell-n
, recalling the definition (
3.3
) of the geometric two-scale expansion, and using
\phi^{0,0}=1
and
\phi^{0,m}=0
for
m>0
,

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)H^{\ell}[\bar{w}]\,=\,\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{w}-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n-1,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-2}}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{n-2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-2}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\phi^{n,\ell-n-1}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{\ell+1-n}\bar{w}+\phi^{n,\ell-n}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{\ell+2-n}\bar{w}\Big)\\
-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}e_{j_{n+1}})+e_{j_{n+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}\Big)\nabla^{n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\\
-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big((e_{j_{n+1}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n-1,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}})\nabla^{n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}+(e_{j_{n+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}e_{j_{n+1}})\nabla^{n+2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+2}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\Big).

As
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{0,m}=0
for all
m\geq 0
, the second right-hand side term can be rewritten as

\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-2}}^{n-1,m}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{n-2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-2}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\,=\,\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-1}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-1-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n,m}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{n-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{w}\\
=\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\nabla\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\odot\nabla^{n-1}\partial_{t}^{m})\nabla\bar{w}-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n,\ell-n}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{n-1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}.

Further rearranging the terms,
and noting that

\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}e_{j_{n+1}})\nabla^{n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}+(e_{j_{n+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\phi^{n,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}e_{j_{n+1}})\nabla^{n+2}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+2}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\\
\,=\,\nabla\cdot\Big(\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\Big),

we are led to

(3.12)

(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{a}\nabla)H^{\ell}[\bar{w}]\,=\,\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{w}-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\nabla\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\odot\nabla^{n-1}\partial_{t}^{m})\nabla\bar{w}\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\phi^{n,\ell-n-1}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{\ell+1-n}\bar{w}+\phi^{n,\ell-n}\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{\ell+2-n}\bar{w}\Big)\\
-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\nabla\cdot\Big(\boldsymbol{a}\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\Big)\\
-\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}+\phi^{n-1,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}}\big)-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n,\ell-n}e_{j_{n}}\Big)\cdot\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}.

The last right-hand side terms can be reformulated in terms of fluxes, cf. Definition
3.1
,

{\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}+\phi^{n-1,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}}\big)-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n,\ell-n}e_{j_{n}}\Big)\cdot\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}}\\
\,=\,\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}q^{n,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\cdot\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-1}\phi^{n+1,\ell-n-2}\odot\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}.

Further noting that we can write

q^{\ell,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}}\cdot\nabla\nabla^{\ell}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}}\bar{w}\,=\,\tilde{q}^{\ell,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}}\cdot\nabla\nabla^{\ell}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}}\bar{w},

in terms of the modified fluxes of Lemma
3.3
,
and then inserting the definition of hyperbolic flux correctors and using the skew-symmetry of
\sigma^{\ell,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}}
, cf. Definition
3.2
and Lemma
3.3
, we deduce

{\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}+\phi^{n-1,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}}\big)-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n,\ell-n}e_{j_{n}}\Big)\cdot\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}}\\
\,=\,-\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\nabla_{j}(\sigma^{n,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}})_{jj_{n+1}}\nabla^{n+1}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n+1}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-1}\phi^{n+1,\ell-n-2}\odot\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}.

Rearranging the terms and further using the skew-symmetry of
\sigma^{\ell,0}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}}
, we get

{\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\boldsymbol{a}\big(\nabla\phi_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n,\ell-n}+\phi^{n-1,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}e_{j_{n}}\big)-\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n-1}}^{n,\ell-n}e_{j_{n}}\Big)\cdot\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}}\\
\,=\,-\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sigma^{n,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\nabla\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\Big)+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-1}\sigma^{n,\ell-n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}:\nabla^{2}\nabla^{n}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}\\
+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-1}\phi^{n+1,\ell-n-2}\odot\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{\ell-n}\bar{w}.

Inserting this into (
3.12
)
yields the conclusion.∎

3.7.
Proof of Lemma
3.8

We split the proof into three steps.

Step
1.
Proof that for all
p\geq 1
there holds

(3.13)

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{W}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\sum_{k=1}^{p}\varepsilon^{2(k-1)}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{k}}\sum_{m\in I^{n}_{k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\\
\,=\,f+\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{k}}\sum_{m\in J^{n}_{k}}\varepsilon^{|m|}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2k}f\\
+\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{p}}\sum_{m\in J^{n}_{p}}\varepsilon^{|m|}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{p}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2(p-1)}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},

where we have defined the following sets of indices, for all
k\geq 1
and
n=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{k})\in[\ell]^{k}:=\{1,\dots,\ell\}^{k}
,

\displaystyle I^{n}_{k}

\displaystyle=\Big\{m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k}):0\leq m_{j}\leq\ell-n_{j}\penalty\ \forall j,\,\sum_{j=1}^{s}m_{j}\geq 2s\penalty\ \forall s<k,\,|m|=2(k-1)\Big\},

\displaystyle J^{n}_{k}

\displaystyle=\Big\{m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k}):0\leq m_{j}\leq\ell-n_{j}\penalty\ \forall j,\,\sum_{j=1}^{s}m_{j}\geq 2s\penalty\ \forall s\leq k\Big\}.

We argue by induction. For
p=1
, the stated identity (
3.13
) reduces to

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,0}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,=\,f+\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=2}^{\ell-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{m}\Big)\nabla\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},

which is a simple reformulation of (
3.5
), keeping in mind that
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}=0
whenever
m
is odd, cf. Definition
3.1
.
Next, we assume that the claim (
3.13
) holds for some
p\geq 1
and we prove that it then also holds at level
p+1
. Let
n\in[\ell]^{p}
and
m\in J_{p}^{n}
be momentarily fixed. As by definition
|m|\geq 2p
, we may use (
3.5
) in the form

\partial_{t}^{|m|-2(p-1)}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}=\partial_{t}^{|m|-2p}\bigg(f+\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-n^{\prime}}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n^{\prime}-1}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{m^{\prime}}\Big)\nabla\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\bigg).

Note that for
1\leq n^{\prime}\leq\ell
and
0\leq m^{\prime}\leq\ell-n^{\prime}
we have the equivalences

\displaystyle(m,m^{\prime})\penalty\ \in\penalty\ \left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}I_{p+1}^{(n,n^{\prime})}&\iff&|m|=2p,\,m^{\prime}=0,\\
J_{p+1}^{(n,n^{\prime})}&\iff&|m|+m^{\prime}\geq 2(p+1).\end{array}\right.

Using these observations, the last term in (
3.13
) can be decomposed as

\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{p}}\sum_{m\in J^{n}_{p}}\varepsilon^{|m|}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{p}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2(p-1)}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\\
\,=\,\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{p}}\sum_{m\in J^{n}_{p}}\varepsilon^{|m|}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{p}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2p}f\\
+\varepsilon^{2p}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{p+1}}\sum_{m\in{I}^{n}_{p+1}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{p+1}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\\
+\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{p+1}}\sum_{m\in J^{n}_{p+1}}\varepsilon^{|m|}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{p+1}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2p}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}.

Inserting this expression into the induction assumption (
3.13
) yields the conclusion.

Step
2. Reformulation.

The definition of the coefficients
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\}_{n}
in the statement leads to

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\ell}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil r/2\rceil}\varepsilon^{2(k-1)}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{k}\atop k+|n|=r+1}\sum_{m\in I_{k}^{n}}\,\prod_{j=1}^{k}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil\ell/2\rceil}\varepsilon^{2(k-1)}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{k}\atop|n|\leq\ell-k+1}\sum_{m\in I_{k}^{n}}\,\prod_{j=1}^{k}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla.

(Here we have used the following observation: for
m=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{k})
and
n=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{k})
with
m_{j}\geq 0
and
n_{j}\geq 1
for all
j
, the restrictions
|m|=2(k-1)
and
k+|n|=r+1
entail
m_{j}+n_{j}\leq r
, thus showing that the index set
I_{k}
in the definition of the coefficients
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\}_{n}
is indeed interchangeable with
I_{k}^{n}
here.)
Choosing
p:=\lceil\frac{\ell}{2}\rceil
in (
3.13
) and inserting the above, we find

(3.14)

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,=\,\nabla\cdot Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\\
+f+\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{k}}\sum_{m\in J^{n}_{k}}\varepsilon^{|m|}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2k}f,

where the remainder
\nabla\cdot Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
is given by

Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,:=\\
\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{p}}\sum_{m\in J^{n}_{p}}\varepsilon^{|m|}\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{1},m_{1}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{1}-1}\big)\nabla\Big(\prod_{j=2}^{p}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2(p-1)}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\\
+\sum_{k=1}^{p}\varepsilon^{2(k-1)}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{k}\atop|n|\geq\ell-k+2}\sum_{m\in I^{n}_{k}}\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{1},m_{1}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{1}-1}\big)\nabla\Big(\prod_{j=2}^{k}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}.

(We use the standard convention
\prod_{j=l}^{k}=1
if
l>k
.)
In order to estimate the remainder
Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
, let us first examine the
\varepsilon
-scaling and the number of derivatives of
\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
appearing in each of the two contributions in its definition:

—

In the first contribution in the definition of
Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
, the terms have scaling
\varepsilon^{|n|+|m|-p}
and involve
|n|+|m|-p+1
space-time derivatives of
\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
, while the condition on
m,n
in the sum ensures the lower bound
|n|+|m|-p\geq|m|\geq 2p\geq\ell
and the upper bound
|n|+|m|-p\leq|n|+p\ell-|n|-p=p(\ell-1)=\lceil\frac{\ell}{2}\rceil(\ell-1)\leq\ell^{2}
.

—

In the second contribution in the definition of
Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
, the terms have scaling
\varepsilon^{|n|+k-2}
and involve
|n|+k-1
derivatives, while the condition on
n,m
in the sum ensures the lower bound
|n|+k-2\geq\ell
and the upper bound
|n|+k-2\leq k\ell+k-2=\lceil\frac{\ell}{2}\rceil(\ell+1)-2\leq\ell^{2}
.

Hence, in view of Lemma
3.4
, we deduce for
\varepsilon\ll 1
, for all
r\geq 0
,

|\langle D\rangle^{r}Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}|\,\lesssim\,(\varepsilon C\ell)^{\ell}|\langle D\rangle^{r+\ell}\langle\varepsilon CD\rangle^{\ell^{2}}D\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}|.

Likewise, the definition of
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\}_{n}
in the statement leads to

\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla\\
\,=\,\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil\ell/2\rceil-1}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{k}}\sum_{m\in J_{k}^{n}\atop|n|+|m|\leq\ell+k-1}\varepsilon^{|m|}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2k},

which, inserted into (
3.14
), yields

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{W}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,=\,f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f+\nabla\cdot(Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}+R_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}),

where the additional remainder
\nabla\cdot R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}
(which is not zero only for
\ell\geq 3
) is given by

R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}\,=\,\sum_{k=1}^{{p-1}}\sum_{n\in[\ell]^{k}}\sum_{m\in J_{k}^{n}\atop|n|+|m|\geq\ell+k}\varepsilon^{|m|}\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{1},m_{1}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{1}-1}\big)\\
\times\nabla\Big(\prod_{j=2}^{k}\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n_{j},m_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)\partial_{t}^{|m|-2k}f.

Again, in order to estimate this remainder, we first check the
\varepsilon
-scaling and the number of derivatives of
f
: the terms have scaling
\varepsilon^{|n|+|m|-k}
and involve
|n|+|m|-k-1
derivatives, while the condition on
n,m
in the sum ensures the lower bound
|n|+|m|-k\geq\ell
and the upper bound
|n|+|m|-k\leq k(\ell-1)\leq(\lceil\frac{\ell}{2}\rceil-1)(\ell-1)\leq\ell^{2}
.
Hence, in view of Lemma
3.4
, we deduce for
\varepsilon\ll 1
, for all
r\geq 0
,

|\langle D\rangle^{r}R_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}|\,\lesssim\,(\varepsilon C\ell)^{\ell}|\langle D\rangle^{r+\ell-1}\langle\varepsilon CD\rangle^{\ell^{2}}f|.

The conclusion with the remainder estimate follows upon setting
E_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}=Q_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}+R_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
.

Step
3. Growth of the revamped coefficients: proof of (
3.6
).

We focus on
\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{p}
, while the estimation of
\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{p}
is obtained similarly.
By definition of
\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{p}
in the statement, together with Lemma
3.4
, we find

\displaystyle|\bar{{\boldsymbol{b}}}^{p}|\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\sum_{(m_{1},\ldots,m_{k})\in I_{k}}\sum_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{k}\geq 1\atop k+|n|=p+1}C^{|n|+|m|}.

For all admissible indices in the above sum, we have
|n|\geq k
, and thus
p+1=k+|n|\geq 2k
, hence
k\leq\tfrac{p+1}{2}
. Moreover, any
m\in I_{k}
satisfies
|m|=2(k-1)
, hence
p+1=k+|n|=|n|+|m|-k+2
. The upper bound for
k
then yields
|n|+|m|\leq\tfrac{3p-1}{2}
, which leads us to the bound

|\bar{{\boldsymbol{b}}}^{p}|\,\leq\,C^{p}\,\sharp\!\left\{r\in\mathbb{N}^{p+1}:\,|r|\leq\tfrac{3p-1}{2}\right\},

and the conclusion
|\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{p}|\leq C^{p}
follows from a simple counting argument with the balls in bins formula.
∎

3.8.
Proof of Lemma
3.9

Let
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
be the solution of a well-posed modification of (
3.7
).
In terms of

(3.15)

f_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}(x)\,:=\,\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{v}^{(\star),\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{n-1}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{m}\Big)\nabla\bar{v}^{(\star),\ell}_{\varepsilon},

we aim to decompose
f_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}=f+\nabla\cdot F_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
for some remainder
F_{\varepsilon}^{(\star),\ell}
satisfying the claimed estimates.
We split the proof into three steps, separately considering the cases
(\star)=\text{(I)}
,
(II)
,
(III)
.

Step
1. High-order filtering:
(\star)=\text{(I)}
.

Applying Lemma
3.8
to (
3.15
), we find

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}\\
=f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}+\nabla\cdot E_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell},

where the remainder satisfies for
\varepsilon\ll 1
, for all
r\geq 0
,

(3.16)

|\langle D\rangle^{r}E_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}|\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C\ell)^{\ell}\Big(|\langle D\rangle^{r+\ell}\langle\varepsilon CD\rangle^{\ell^{2}}D\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}|+|\langle D\rangle^{r+\ell-1}\langle\varepsilon CD\rangle^{\ell^{2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}|\Big).

As
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
is the solution of the well-posed modification of (
3.7
) obtained by high-order filtering in the sense of Lemma
2.10
, we deduce

\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\bigg(f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f\bigg)\\
\,=\,f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}+\nabla\cdot E_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell},

or equivalently,

(3.17)

f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}\\
\,=\,f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f+\nabla\cdot\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell},

with modified remainder

(3.18)

\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}\,:=\,-E_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}-(1-\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla))\triangle^{-1}\nabla\bigg(f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f\bigg).

In order to estimate
f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
, it remains to invert the differential operator

(3.19)

1+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla

in the left-hand side of (
3.17
).
Although it is not invertible in general, we may invert it approximately using a Neumann series truncated at order
O(\varepsilon^{\ell})
. More precisely, we set

\mathcal{O}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}\,:=\,1+\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}(-\varepsilon^{2})^{k}\sum_{n\in[\ell-2]^{k}\atop|n|\leq\ell-k}\penalty\ \prod_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big).

By a simple counting argument with the balls in bins formula, the number of terms in this sum defining
\mathcal{O}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}
can be bounded by

\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}\sharp\{n\in[\ell-2]^{k}:|n|\leq\ell-k\}\,\leq\,C^{\ell},

and therefore, combining this cardinality bound
with the bound
|\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}|\leq C^{n}
, we easily deduce for any function
g
, for all
r\geq 0
,

(3.20)

|\langle D\rangle^{r}\mathcal{O}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}g|\leq C^{\ell}|\langle D\rangle^{r}\langle\varepsilon CD\rangle^{\ell}g|.

Next, we check that
\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
is indeed an approximate inverse for the differential operator in (
3.19
): by definition of
\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
, working out cancellations, we find for any function
g
,

\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\bigg(1+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla\bigg)g\,=\,g+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}g,

where the remainder is explicitly given by

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}g\,:=\,

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=2}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}(-\varepsilon^{2})^{k}\sum_{n\in[\ell-2]^{k}\atop\ell+1-k\leq|n|\leq n_{k}+\ell+1-k}\penalty\ \prod_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)g

\displaystyle-(-\varepsilon^{2})^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor+1}\sum_{n\in[\ell-2]^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor+1}\atop|n|\leq n_{k}+\ell-\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}\penalty\ \prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor+1}\Big(\nabla\cdot\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n_{j}}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n_{j}-1}\big)\nabla\Big)g,

and can be estimated as follows: for all
r\geq 0
, using
|\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}|\leq C^{n}
, we have that

(3.21)

|\langle D\rangle^{r}\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}g|\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}|\langle D\rangle^{r+2\ell}g|.

Applying
\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
to both sides of (
3.17
), we then get

f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}\,=\,f+\nabla\cdot F_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell},\qquad F_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}\,:=\,\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}(f-f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell})+\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell},

and the desired remainder estimate follows by combining (
3.15
), (
3.16
), (
3.18
), (
3.20
), and (
3.21
), together with the a priori estimate of Lemma
2.10
(ii) for
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
. Note that the remainder term that is local with respect to
f
can also be bounded with an
\operatorname{L}^{1}(0,t)
-norm instead of an
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(0,t)
-norm up to loosing a time derivative.

Step
2. Higher-order regularization:
(\star)=\text{(II)}
.

As
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell}
is the solution of the well-posed modification of (
3.7
) obtained by higher-order regularization in the sense of Lemma
2.10
, we get instead of (
3.17
),

f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell}+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell}\\
\,=\,f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f+\nabla\cdot\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell},

with remainder

\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell}\,:=\,-E_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell}+\kappa_{\ell}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{\ell}\nabla\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell},

where
E_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell}
satisfies the corresponding estimate (
3.16
).
Now applying the same approximate inverse operator
\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}
to both sides of this identity, we get

f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell}\,=\,f+\nabla\cdot F_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell},\qquad F_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell}\,:=\,\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}(f-f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell})+\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(II)},\ell},

and the desired remainder estimate follows similarly.

Step
3. Boussinesq trick:
(\star)=\text{(III)}
.

By definition,
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}
is the solution of the well-posed modification of (
3.7
) obtained by Boussinesq trick in the sense of Lemma
2.10
, that is,

(3.22)

\partial_{t}^{2}\Big(1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}\\
-\nabla\cdot\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\kappa_{n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(\tfrac{\nabla}{|\nabla|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{n-1}\bigg)\nabla\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}\\
\,=\,\Big(1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)\bigg(f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla f\bigg),

with
\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}=f=0
for
t\leq 0
. We recall that the coefficients
\{\kappa_{l}\}_{l}
are defined in (
2.22
) and that well-posedness is indeed ensured by Lemma
2.10
(ii).
Using the identity

\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\Big(\kappa_{n}\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(\tfrac{\nabla}{|\nabla|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{n-1}\\
=\Big(1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\Big)\\
-\sum_{n=\ell+1}^{2\ell-1}\Big(\sum_{l=n+1-\ell}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(\tfrac{\nabla}{|\nabla|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{n-1},

the above equation (
3.22
) can be alternatively written as

\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}-\nabla\cdot\Big(\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon\nabla)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}\,=\,f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla f-\nabla\cdot G_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},

in terms of

G_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\,:=\,\sum_{n=\ell+1}^{2\ell-1}\Big(\sum_{l=n+1-\ell}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n+1-l}\odot(\tfrac{\nabla}{|\nabla|})^{n-l}\Big)(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{n-1}\Big(1+\sum_{l=2}^{\ell}\kappa_{l}(\varepsilon|\nabla|)^{l-1}\Big)^{-1}\nabla\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell},

where the inverse operator is obviously well-defined as
\kappa_{l}\geq 0
for all
l
.
We then get, instead of (
3.17
),

f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{n-1}\Big)\nabla f_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}\\
\,=\,f+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla f+\nabla\cdot\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell},

with remainder

\hat{E}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}\,:=\,-E_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}-G_{\varepsilon}^{\ell},

where
E_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(III)},\ell}
satisfies the corresponding estimate (
3.16
).
The conclusion then follows similarly as in the first two steps.∎

4.
Relating spectral to hyperbolic correctors

This last section is devoted to the proof of (
1.12
).
It relies on an algorithmic procedure to relate spectral and hyperbolic correctors. We split the proof into two steps.

Step
1. Reformulation of the hyperbolic two-scale expansion: for
\varepsilon\ll 1
, we have for all
\ell\geq 1
,

(4.1)

\bigg\|H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell;t}]-\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\varepsilon^{n}\mathring{\psi}_{\ell}^{n}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell;t}_{\varepsilon}\\
-\varepsilon^{3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-n-3}\varepsilon^{n+2m}\mathring{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f^{t}\bigg\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{C\ell}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

where for each
n,m
the correctors
\mathring{\psi}_{\ell}^{n}
and
\mathring{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,m}
are suitable linear combinations of hyperbolic correctors
\{\phi^{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\}_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}
with coefficients involving
\ell
and
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n^{\prime}},\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n^{\prime}}\}_{n^{\prime}}
.

Comparing the effective equation in the spectral and in the geometric approach, cf. (
2.3
) and (
3.7
), and using the well-posed modification by high-order filtering, we get

(4.2)

\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}\,=\,\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\ell-2}\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{k}\odot(\varepsilon D)^{k-1}\Big)\nabla\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}.

Inserting this into the definition (
3.3
) of the geometric two-scale expansion
H_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]
, and rearranging terms, we get

(4.3)

H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]\,=\,\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\varepsilon^{n+m}z^{n,m}_{\ell}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}+R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,1}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}],

where for each
n,m
the corrector
z^{n,m}_{\ell}
is some linear combination of hyperbolic correctors
\{\phi^{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\}_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}
with coefficients involving
\ell
and
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n^{\prime}}\}_{n^{\prime}}
, and where the remainder term is given by

R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,1}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]\,:=\\
\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{\ell-n}\sum_{k=1}^{\ell-2}\mathds{1}_{n+m+k\geq\ell}\,\varepsilon^{n+m+k+1}\phi^{n,m}_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})(\bar{\boldsymbol{c}}^{k}\odot D^{k-1}):\nabla^{2}\nabla_{j_{1}\ldots j_{n}}^{n}\partial_{t}^{m}\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}.

By construction, we note that
z^{0,0}_{\ell}=\phi^{0,0}=1
,
z^{0,m}_{\ell}=\phi^{0,m}=0
for all
m\geq 1
, and that
z^{n,m}_{\ell}
does not depend on
\ell
provided
n+m<\ell
. We emphasize that the
z^{n,m}_{\ell}
’s do a priori not have vanishing average.
Using Lemma
3.4
together with (
3.6
), and using the Sobolev embedding in form of (
2.40
) to bound products with correctors, the remainder can be estimated as follows, for
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

\|R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,1}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a-1}D\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and thus, by the a priori estimate of Lemma
2.10
(ii),

(4.4)

\|R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,1}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell;t}]\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a-{1}}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Next, using the equation (
2.3
) for
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
, we shall proceed to remove the time derivatives appearing in the geometric two-scale expansion (
4.3
).
For that purpose, arguing by induction and successively separating terms of order
\varepsilon^{\ell+1}
or higher, we show that the equation for
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
yields for all
m\geq 1
,

(4.5)

\partial_{t}^{2m}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\sum_{1\leq\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{m}\leq\ell\atop|\beta|\leq m+\ell}\varepsilon^{|\beta|-m}\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}\\
=\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\partial_{t}^{2(m-1)}f+\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\sum_{1\leq\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k}\leq\ell\atop|\beta|\leq k+\ell}\varepsilon^{|\beta|-k}\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\partial_{t}^{2(m-k-1)}f\\
+\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{\beta\in L_{k}}\varepsilon^{|\beta|-k}\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\partial_{t}^{2(m-k)}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon},

in terms of the index sets

L_{k}\,:=\,\Big\{\beta=(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{k}):1\leq\beta_{j}\leq\ell\penalty\ \forall j,\penalty\ \sum_{j=1}^{s}\beta_{j}\leq s+\ell\penalty\ \forall s<k,\penalty\ |\beta|>k+\ell\Big\},

where for all
k\geq 1
and
\beta=(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k})
we use the short-hand notation

(4.6)

\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\,:=\,\prod_{j=1}^{k}\nabla\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{\beta_{j}}\odot\nabla^{\beta_{j}-1})\nabla.

We prove identity (
4.5
) by induction.
First, for
m=1
, it simply coincides with (the filtered version of) equation (
2.3
) for
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
.
Next, assuming that (
4.5
) holds for some
m\geq 1
, applying
\partial_{t}^{2}
to both sides of the identity, and using the equation for
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
to replace
\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}
in the left-hand side, we find

\partial_{t}^{2(m+1)}\bar{u}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}-\sum_{1\leq\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{m}\leq\ell\atop|\beta|\leq m+\ell}\varepsilon^{|\beta|-m}\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\varepsilon^{n-1}\nabla\cdot(\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}\odot\nabla^{n-1})\nabla\Big)\bar{u}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}\\
=\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\partial_{t}^{2m}f+\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k}\leq\ell\atop|\beta|\leq k+\ell}\varepsilon^{|\beta|-k}\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\partial_{t}^{2(m-k)}f\\
+\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{\beta\in L_{k}}\varepsilon^{|\beta|-k}\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\partial_{t}^{2(m-k+1)}\bar{u}^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}.

After suitably splitting powers of
\varepsilon
in the left-hand side, this indeed proves identity (
4.5
) with
m
replaced by
m+1
.

Now inserting (
4.5
) into the two-scale expansion (
4.3
) in order to replace time derivatives, and again separating terms of order
\varepsilon^{\ell+1}
or higher, we are led to

(4.7)

H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]\,=\,\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\varepsilon^{n}z^{n,0}_{\ell}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}\\
+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{2m=2}^{\ell-n}\sum_{1\leq\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{m}\leq\ell\atop n+m+|\beta|\leq\ell}\varepsilon^{n+m+|\beta|}z^{n,2m}_{\ell}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}\\
+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{2m=2}^{\ell-n}\varepsilon^{n+2m}z^{n,2m}_{\ell}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{2(m-1)}f\\
+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{2m=2}^{\ell-n}\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}\sum_{1\leq\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{j}\leq\ell\atop n+2m+|\beta|\leq j+\ell}\varepsilon^{n+2m+|\beta|-j}z^{n,2m}_{\ell}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{2(m-j-1)}f\\
+R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,1}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]+R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,2}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]+R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,3}[f],

where the last two remainder terms take the form

\displaystyle R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,2}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{2m=2}^{\ell-n}\sum_{j=2}^{m}\sum_{\beta\in L_{j}}\varepsilon^{n+2m+|\beta|-j}z_{\ell}^{n,2m}\left(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\odot\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{2(m-j)}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{2m=2}^{\ell-n}\sum_{1\leq\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{m}\leq\ell\atop|\beta|\leq m+\ell,n+m+|\beta|>\ell}\varepsilon^{n+m+|\beta|}z_{\ell}^{n,2m}\left(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\odot\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,3}[f]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{2m=2}^{\ell-n}\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}\sum_{1\leq\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{j}\leq\ell\atop|\beta|\leq j+\ell,\penalty\ n+2m+|\beta|>j+\ell}\varepsilon^{n+2m+|\beta|-j}z_{\ell}^{n,2m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\odot\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\bar{B}^{\beta}(\nabla)\nabla^{n}\partial_{t}^{2(m-j-1)}f.

Equivalently, in view of (
4.6
), this can be reformulated as

(4.8)

H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]\,=\\
\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\varepsilon^{n}\mathring{\psi}_{\ell}^{n}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell}_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-n-3}\varepsilon^{n+2m}\mathring{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\\
+R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,1}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]+R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,2}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}]+R^{\ell}_{\varepsilon,3}[f],

where for each
n,m
the correctors
\mathring{\psi}^{n}_{\ell}
and
\mathring{\zeta}^{n,2m}_{\ell}
are suitable linear combinations of
\{z^{n^{\prime},2m^{\prime}}\}_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}
with coefficients involving
\ell
and
\{\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n^{\prime}}\}_{n^{\prime}}
.
We emphasize that
\mathring{\psi}^{n}_{\ell}
and
\mathring{\zeta}^{n,2m}_{\ell}
do a priori not have vanishing average.

We turn to the estimation of the last two remainder terms in (
4.8
).
Recalling the way that
z^{n,2m}
depends on
\{\phi^{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\}_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}
and on
\{{\boldsymbol{c}}^{n^{\prime}}\}_{n^{\prime}}
, using Lemma
3.4
together with (
3.6
), and using again the Sobolev embedding in form of (
2.40
) to bound products with correctors, we find for
\varepsilon\ll 1
and
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

\|R_{\varepsilon,2}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell}]\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{3\ell+a-1}D\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and thus, further combining this with the a priori estimate of Lemma
2.10
(ii),

\|R_{\varepsilon,2}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell;t}]\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{3\ell+a-1}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Similarly, we find for
\varepsilon\ll 1
and
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

\|R_{\varepsilon,3}^{\ell}[f]\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{2\ell+a-1}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Finally, we recall that the cut-off
\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla)
can be removed in the right-hand side of (
4.8
) up to higher-order errors: for
a>\frac{d}{2}
,

{\bigg\|\varepsilon^{3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-n-3}\varepsilon^{n+2m}\mathring{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot(1-\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla))\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\bigg\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\\
\,\leq\,\varepsilon^{3}C^{\ell}\|(1-\chi(\varepsilon^{\alpha}\nabla))\langle D\rangle^{\ell+a-1}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{\ell+{\lceil\frac{\ell}{\alpha}\rceil}+a-1}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
{\,\leq\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{\ell+{\lceil\frac{\ell}{\alpha}\rceil}+a}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}}.

Inserting these bounds together with (
4.4
) into (
4.8
), the claim (
4.1
) follows.

Step
2. Conclusion.

In view of (
4.1
), the result of Theorem
2
yields

\bigg\|u_{\varepsilon}^{t}-\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\varepsilon^{n}\mathring{\psi}_{\ell}^{n}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell;t}_{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon^{3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-n-3}\varepsilon^{n+2m}\mathring{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f^{t}\bigg\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\,\lesssim\,(\varepsilon C\ell)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{C\ell^{2}}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Now we compare this with the result of Theorem
1
:
using (
2.7
) to expand
\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)
in the spectral two-scale expansion (
1.4
), discarding terms of order
O(\varepsilon^{\ell})
, and using Lemma
2.10
(ii) to estimate the latter, the result (
1.6
) of Theorem
1
leads us to

(4.9)

\bigg\|\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\varepsilon^{n}\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{n}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n}\bar{u}^{\text{(I)},\ell;t}_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-3}\sum_{2m=0}^{\ell-n-3}\varepsilon^{n+2m}\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}(\tfrac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})\odot\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f^{t}\bigg\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\,\lesssim\,(\varepsilon C\ell)^{\ell}\langle t\rangle\|\langle D\rangle^{C\ell^{2}}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

in terms of the corrector differences

\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{n}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\gamma_{\ell}^{k}\otimes_{s}\psi^{n-k}-\mathring{\psi}_{\ell}^{n},\qquad\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\gamma_{\ell}^{k}\otimes_{s}\zeta^{n-k,2m}-\mathring{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m},

where
\otimes_{s}
stands for symmetric tensor product.
We claim that (
4.9
) entails
\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{n}=0
for all
n\leq\ell-1
,
\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}=0
for all
n+2m+3\leq\ell-1
, and
\nabla\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{\ell}=0
and
\nabla\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}=0
for
n+2m+3=\ell
.
We argue by induction and prove:

(4.10)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\text{for all $j\leq\ell-1$}&:&\text{$\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{j}=0$ and $\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}=0$ for $n+2m+3=j$},\\
\text{for $j=\ell$}&:&\text{$\nabla\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{\ell}=0$ and $\nabla\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}=0$ for $n+2m+3=\ell$.}\end{array}\right.

Assume that this result is known to hold for
j<j_{0}
, given some
j_{0}\leq\ell-1
.
Using the a priori estimates of Lemma
2.10
(ii), first note that we have
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{(I)},\ell}\to\bar{u}
in
C^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R};H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
, where
\bar{u}
is the solution of the standard homogenized wave equation (
1.3
). Given
h\in C_{\operatorname{per}}^{\infty}(Q)
, multiplying the expression in the left-hand side of (
4.9
) by
\varepsilon^{-j_{0}}h(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon})
, and passing to the limit
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
in the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-norm, we then get

\mathbb{E}\big[h\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{j_{0}}\big]\odot\nabla^{j_{0}}\bar{u}+\sum_{n+2m+3=j_{0}}\mathbb{E}\big[h\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}\big]\odot\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}f\,=\,0.

As
\bar{u}
satisfies the homogenized wave equation (
1.3
),
applying the wave operator
\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\nabla
to this pointwise identity leads us to

\mathbb{E}\big[h\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{j_{0}}\big]\odot\nabla^{j_{0}}f+\sum_{n+2m+3=j_{0}}\mathbb{E}\big[h\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}\big]\odot\nabla^{n+1}\partial_{t}^{2m}(\partial_{t}^{2}-\nabla\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}\nabla)f\,=\,0.

Choosing for instance
f^{t}(x)=\exp(-(t^{2}+|x|^{2}))
for
t\geq 1
, it is easily deduced by induction, by a linear independence argument, that
\mathbb{E}\big[h\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}^{j_{0}}\big]=0
and
\mathbb{E}\big[h\widetilde{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}\big]=0
for all
n,m
with
n+2m+3=j_{0}
. As
h\in C^{\infty}_{\operatorname{per}}(Q)
is arbitrary, we deduce that the claim (
4.10
) also holds for
j=j_{0}
. The same argument can be adapted to the case
j_{0}=\ell
, rather starting from the estimate on the
H^{1}
-norm in (
4.9
). This ends the proof of (
4.10
).

In other words, we have thus proved that, given
\ell\geq 1
, for all
j\leq\ell-1
and for all
n,m
with
n+m+3\leq\ell-1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{\psi}_{\ell}^{j}=\sum_{k=0}^{j}\gamma_{\ell}^{k}\otimes_{s}\psi^{j-k},\qquad\mathring{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\gamma_{\ell}^{k}\otimes_{s}\zeta^{n-k,2m},

while for
j=\ell
and for all
n,m
with
n+m+3=\ell
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}\mathring{\psi}_{\ell}^{\ell}=\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}\gamma_{\ell}^{k}\otimes_{s}\nabla_{i}\psi^{\ell-k},\qquad\nabla_{i}\mathring{\zeta}_{\ell}^{n,2m}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\gamma_{\ell}^{k}\otimes_{s}\nabla_{i}\zeta^{n-k,2m}.

Inserting this back into (
4.1
), using (
2.7
) to reconstruct
\gamma_{\ell}(\varepsilon\nabla)
, discarding terms of order
O(\varepsilon^{\ell})
, and using Lemma
2.10
(ii) to estimate the latter,
we may then recognize the definition (
1.4
) of the spectral two-scale expansion, to the effect of

\|H^{\ell}_{\varepsilon}[\bar{v}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell;t}]-S_{\varepsilon}^{\ell}[\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\text{(I)},\ell;t},f]\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim\,(\varepsilon C)^{\ell}\|\langle D\rangle^{C\ell}f\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.

Combined with the result (
1.6
) of Theorem
1
, this yields the conclusion (
1.12
).∎

Appendix A
Correctors in the random setting

This section is devoted to the definition and bounds on spectral and hyperbolic correctors in the random setting.
As in the elliptic case
[
18
,
4
,
21
,
15
]
, the main difference with the periodic setting is that only a finite number of correctors can be defined, depending both on space dimension and on mixing properties of the coefficient field.
For simplicity, we focus on the Gaussian setting of Definition
1.3
.
The corrector estimates below were first obtained in
[
19
,
20
,
18
]
for the first corrector in the elliptic setting.
A proof of the present statement follows from applying iteratively the annealed Calderón-Zygmund estimates of
[
15
,
11
]
; see in particular a similar argument in
[
15
, Proof of Proposition 2.2]
. Note that the present result corrects inaccuracies of the corresponding statement given in
[
5
, Proposition C.4]
for spectral correctors.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
\boldsymbol{a}
be Gaussian with parameter
\beta>0
in the sense of Definition
1.3
.
We then define
\ell_{*}\,:=\,\lceil\tfrac{\beta\wedge d}{2}\rceil
and

(A.1)

\mu_{n}^{*}(x)\,:=\,\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}1&:&n<\ell_{*},\\
\log(2+|x|)^{\frac{1}{2}}&:&n=\ell_{*},\penalty\ \beta>d,\penalty\ \text{$d$ even},\\
&&\text{or }n=\ell_{*},\penalty\ \beta<d,\penalty\ \beta\in 2\mathbb{N},\\
\log(2+|x|)&:&n=\ell_{*},\penalty\ \beta=d,\penalty\ \text{$d$ even},\\
\langle x\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}&:&n=\ell_{*},\penalty\ \beta>d,\penalty\ \text{$d$ odd},\\
\langle x\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\log(2+|x|)^{\frac{1}{2}}&:&n=\ell_{*},\penalty\ \beta=d,\penalty\ \text{$d$ odd},\\
\langle x\rangle^{n-\frac{\beta}{2}}&:&n=\ell_{*},\penalty\ \beta<d,\penalty\ \beta\notin 2\mathbb{N}.\end{array}\right.

(i)

Spectral correctors:

The correctors
\{\psi^{n},\sigma^{n}\}_{0\leq n<\ell_{*}}
and
\{\zeta^{n,m},\tau^{n,m}\}_{n+2m<\ell_{*}-3}
can be uniquely defined by the corrector equations of Section
2.1
as centered stationary random fields, and in addition the correctors
\psi^{n},\sigma^{n}
with
n=\ell_{*}
and
\zeta^{n,m},\tau^{n,m}
with
n+2m=\ell_{*}-3
can be uniquely defined as (non-stationary) random fields with centered stationary gradient.
The homogenized coefficient
\bar{\boldsymbol{b}}^{n}
is well-defined for
0\leq n\leq\ell_{*}
.
Moreover, the following moment bounds hold for all
q<\infty
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\qquad\Big\|\Big(\fint_{B(x)}|(\psi^{n},\sigma^{n})|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\penalty\displaystyle\lesssim_{q}\penalty\ \mu_{n}^{*}(x),\quad

\displaystyle\text{for $0\leq n\leq\ell_{*}$},

\displaystyle\qquad\Big\|\Big(\fint_{B(x)}|(\nabla\psi^{n},\nabla\sigma^{n})|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\penalty\displaystyle\lesssim_{q}\penalty\ 1,\quad

\displaystyle\text{for $0\leq n\leq\ell_{*}$},

\displaystyle\quad\Big\|\Big(\fint_{B(x)}|(\zeta^{n,m},\tau^{n,m})|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\penalty\displaystyle\lesssim_{q}\penalty\ \mu^{*}_{n+2m+3}(x),\quad

\displaystyle\text{for $n+2m\leq\ell_{*}-3$},

\displaystyle\qquad\Big\|\Big(\fint_{B(x)}|(\nabla\zeta^{n,m},\nabla\tau^{n,m})|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\penalty\displaystyle\lesssim_{q}\penalty\ 1,\quad

\displaystyle\text{for $n+2m\leq\ell_{*}-3$}.

(ii)

Hyperbolic correctors:
The correctors
\{\phi^{n,m},\sigma^{n,m}\}_{n+m<\ell_{*}}
can be uniquely defined by the corrector equations of Section
3.1
as centered stationary random fields, and in addition the correctors
\phi^{n,m},\sigma^{n,m}
with
n+m=\ell_{*}
can be uniquely defined as (non-stationary) random fields with centered stationary gradient. The homogenized coefficient
\bar{\boldsymbol{a}}^{n,m}
is well-defined for
n+m\leq\ell_{*}
.
Moreover, for
n+m\leq\ell_{*}
, the following moment bounds hold for all
q<\infty
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\qquad\Big\|\Big(\fint_{B(x)}|(\phi^{n,m},\sigma^{n,m})|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lesssim_{q}\penalty\ \mu_{n+m}^{*}(x),

\displaystyle\qquad\Big\|\Big(\fint_{B(x)}|(\nabla\phi^{n,m},\nabla\sigma^{n,m})|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lesssim_{q}\penalty\ 1.\qed

Appendix B
A priori estimates for the wave equation

We state the following general a priori estimates for linear wave equations, which are used throughout; a short proof is included for convenience. In contrast with the situation in the elliptic setting, we emphasize that putting the impulse in divergence form essentially only brings an improvement when estimating the
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-norm, and not the energy norm.

Lemma B.1

(A priori estimates)
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a self-adjoint operator on
\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfying the bound
-\triangle\leq\mathcal{L}\leq-C_{0}\triangle
for some constant
C_{0}<\infty
. Given

F_{1}\in\operatorname{L}^{1}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{+};\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
F_{2},F_{3}\in W^{1,1}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{+};\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
with
F_{2}|_{t=0}=F_{3}|_{t=0}=0
, let
z
be the solution of the wave equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}^{2}+\mathcal{L})z\,=\,F_{1}+\nabla\cdot F_{2}+\partial_{t}F_{3}\quad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$},\\
z|_{t=0}=\partial_{t}z|_{t=0}=0.\end{array}\right.

Then, for all
t\geq 0
, we have

\|z^{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim_{C_{0}}\,t\|F_{1}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}+\|(F_{2},F_{3})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

and

\|Dz^{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim_{C_{0}}\,\|(F_{1},\partial_{t}F_{2},\partial_{t}F_{3})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{1}((0,t);\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}.\qed

Proof.

The assumption
-\triangle\leq\mathcal{L}\leq-C_{0}\triangle
entails that
\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}
defines a bounded linear operator
\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with bounded inverse. We may therefore define
\tilde{F}_{2}
as the solution of
\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}\tilde{F}_{2}=\nabla\cdot F_{2}
, which satisfies
\|\tilde{F}_{2}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{C_{0}}\|F_{2}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
The solution
z
of the wave equation can then be represented in terms of Duhamel’s formula,

z^{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\sin((t-s)\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})}{\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}\,(F_{1}^{s}+\partial_{s}F_{3}^{s})\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sin((t-s)\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})\,\tilde{F}_{2}^{s}\,ds.

Integrating by parts in the integral for
F_{3}
, with
F_{3}|_{t=0}=0
, this can be rewritten as

z^{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\sin((t-s)\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})}{\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}\,F_{1}^{s}\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sin((t-s)\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})\,\tilde{F}_{2}^{s}\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\cos((t-s)\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})\,F_{3}^{s}\,ds,

and the stated
\operatorname{L}^{2}
-estimate follows from spectral calculus.
For the energy estimate, we rather use energy conservation in form of

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(|\partial_{t}z|^{2}+z\mathcal{L}z+2F_{2}\cdot\nabla z\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\partial_{t}z)\,(\partial^{2}_{t}+\mathcal{L})z+\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}F_{2}\cdot\nabla z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\partial_{t}z)\big(F_{1}+\partial_{t}F_{3}+\nabla\cdot F_{2}\big)+\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}F_{2}\cdot\nabla z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\partial_{t}z)\big(F_{1}+\partial_{t}F_{3}\big)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{t}F_{2}\cdot\nabla z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|Dz\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|(F_{1},\partial_{t}F_{2},\partial_{t}F_{3})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and the conclusion follows.
∎
